# Collocation listening scripts: Stage 4

Listening scripts for the collocations list (21-list-collocations.json), one per chunk, in course order. Each script is a short scene where that chunk's collocations come up several times, plus a few from earlier chunks for review. For each one: make the audio from the Japanese (one voice per speaker, the same voices in every script; if a voice misreads a kanji, use the kana version of that line), listen without looking at any text, answer the questions out loud, then check the answers and the romaji transcript. Say-it questions (type respond) have no choices: a character says the cue line and you answer in Japanese. Words outside the versatility list (up to the script's stage) and the collocations so far are listed as new words.

## 70. Breakfast next door

*Breakfast · Polite monologue · 10 lines · Satou-san*

Seven in the morning. Satou-san, the elderly man next door, leaves a voicemail for Kenta, who told him yesterday that he never eats breakfast. Yesterday Satou-san also baked his first ever loaf of bread, and it came out far too big for one person.

**Collocations:** *ocha o ireru* (make tea); *satou o ireru* (put sugar in, add sugar); *pan o yaku* (toast bread; bake bread); *asagohan o nuku* (skip breakfast).

**Review:** *shigoto ni iku* (go to work); *oyu o wakasu* (boil water).

### Audio script

| Speaker | Voice |
|---|---|
| Satou-san | male, 70s, polite, kind, unhurried |

| # | Speaker | Japanese | Kana (if the voice misreads a kanji) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Satou-san | もしもし、ケンタくん？隣の佐藤です。朝早くにすみませんね。 | もしもし、けんたくん？となりのさとうです。あさはやくにすみませんね。 |
| 2 | Satou-san | 昨日、ケンタくん、朝ご飯はいつも抜いてるって言ってましたよね。 | きのう、けんたくん、あさごはんはいつもぬいてるっていってましたよね。 |
| 3 | Satou-san | 実は私、昨日初めてパンを焼いてみたんです。 | じつはわたし、きのうはじめてパンをやいてみたんです。 |
| 4 | Satou-san | それがね、すごく大きいのができちゃって。一人じゃ全部は食べられないんですよ。 | それがね、すごくおおきいのができちゃって。ひとりじゃぜんぶはたべられないんですよ。 |
| 5 | Satou-san | だから、仕事に行く前に、うちに寄っていきませんか？朝ご飯を抜くのは、体によくないですからね。 | だから、しごとにいくまえに、うちによっていきませんか？あさごはんをぬくのは、からだによくないですからね。 |
| 6 | Satou-san | ケンタくんが来たら、すぐそのパンを焼いて、お茶も入れますよ。 | けんたくんがきたら、すぐそのパンをやいて、おちゃもいれますよ。 |
| 7 | Satou-san | あ、そういえば、ケンタくんはコーヒーにお砂糖を三つも入れますよね。 | あ、そういえば、けんたくんはコーヒーにおさとうをみっつもいれますよね。 |
| 8 | Satou-san | 残念ですけど、うちにはコーヒーがないんです。その代わり、おいしいお茶を入れますから。 | ざんねんですけど、うちにはコーヒーがないんです。そのかわり、おいしいおちゃをいれますから。 |
| 9 | Satou-san | でも、お茶にはお砂糖、入れないでくださいね。 | でも、おちゃにはおさとう、いれないでくださいね。 |
| 10 | Satou-san | じゃあ、お湯を沸かして待ってますね。今日は朝ご飯、抜いちゃだめですよ。 | じゃあ、おゆをわかしてまってますね。きょうはあさごはん、ぬいちゃだめですよ。 |

### Questions

1. **Why does Satou-san call Kenta?**  
   satou-san wa, doushite kenta-kun ni denwa shita n desu ka? (佐藤さんは、どうしてケンタくんに電話したんですか？)
   - A. He has run out of coffee, and he asks Kenta to bring some over before work.
   - B. His first loaf of bread is too big to finish alone, so he invites Kenta, who usually skips breakfast, to come and eat before work.
   - C. His first loaf of bread went wrong, so he asks Kenta, who's good at cooking, to come and show him how.

2. **Tanaka-san comes back into the office from outside and says: "Brr, that was cold. It's windy out there." Offer to make him a hot cup of tea.** *(say it)*  
   aa, samukatta. soto wa kaze ga tsuyoi desu ne. (ああ、寒かった。外は風が強いですね。)

3. **Mai, your coworker and friend, has made you a coffee and says: "I made coffee. Is black OK?" You like it sweet. Ask her to put some sugar in.** *(say it)*  
   koohii ireta yo. burakku de ii? (コーヒー入れたよ。ブラックでいい？)

4. **What did Satou-san do with bread yesterday, and what will he do with it when Kenta arrives?**  
   satou-san wa, kinou pan o dou shimashita ka? kenta-kun ga kitara, dou shimasu ka? (佐藤さんは、昨日パンをどうしましたか？ケンタくんが来たら、どうしますか？)
   - A. Yesterday he made toast for the first time, and when Kenta comes he'll make more toast.
   - B. Yesterday he baked bread for the first time, and when Kenta comes he'll bake a fresh loaf.
   - C. Yesterday he baked bread for the first time, and when Kenta comes he'll toast some of it.

5. **What does Satou-san say at the very end of his message?**  
   satou-san wa saigo ni nan to iimashita ka? (佐藤さんは最後に何と言いましたか？)
   - A. *kyou wa asagohan, nuicha dame desu yo.* (No skipping breakfast today!)
   - B. *kyou wa asagohan, tobashicha dame desu yo.* (No jumping over breakfast today!)
   - C. *kyou wa asagohan, yasunja dame desu yo.* (No taking the day off from breakfast today!)

### New words

- **tonari** (隣): next door (tonari no satou = Satou from next door)
- **-kun** (くん): name suffix for a younger man or boy (kenta-kun)
- **burakku** (ブラック): black (coffee with no sugar or milk)
- **tobasu** (飛ばす): skip over (pages, questions, steps); not used for meals

<details>
<summary>Answers and transcript</summary>

1. **B**. Line 2: Kenta said yesterday that he always skips breakfast (itsumo nuiteru). Lines 3-4: Satou-san baked bread for the first time and it came out huge, too much to eat alone. Line 5: so he invites Kenta to drop by before work (shigoto ni iku mae ni, uchi ni yotte ikimasen ka?). A: he does say he has no coffee (line 8), but he offers tea instead; he doesn't ask Kenta for anything. C: the bread didn't go wrong, it just came out too big (sugoku ookii no ga dekichatte), and Satou-san is the one doing the cooking.
2. Offer tea with ocha o ireru, politely, since he's your manager. iremashou ka? means 'shall I make some?': atsui ocha, iremashou ka? Not ocha o tsukurimashou ka. For example: *otsukaresama desu. atsui ocha, iremashou ka?* (Welcome back. Shall I make you a hot cup of tea?); *jaa, ocha iremasu ne. chotto matte kudasai.* (I'll make you some tea, then. Just a moment.). Satou-san offers tea the same way: ocha mo iremasu yo (line 6) and oishii ocha o iremasu kara (line 8). For a cup or a pot of tea or coffee the verb is ireru, which here means brewing it by pouring on hot water. English 'make' leads to ocha o tsukuru, but tsukuru is for a big jug made in advance, like a pitcher of cold barley tea. Handing the cup to a guest afterwards is ocha o dasu; ireru is the making.
3. Ask for sugar with satou o ireru; Japanese has no 'take' here: satou irete kureru? For example: *arigatou. demo, satou irete kureru?* (Thanks. Could you put some sugar in, though?); *a, gomen. satou futatsu irete moraeru?* (Oh, sorry. Could I get two sugars in it?). Sugar going into a drink is ireru in its plain 'put in' sense: Satou-san says koohii ni osatou o mittsu mo iremasu yo ne (line 7) and ocha ni wa osatou, irenaide kudasai ne (line 9). English 'I take sugar' has no match: satou o toru means picking the sugar up (satou totte = pass me the sugar), not having it in your coffee. Mai's koohii ireta is the other ireru, brewing the cup. With a friend plain satou is fine; Satou-san says osatou because he's speaking politely. (satou, sugar, has the same sounds as his name, only the pitch differs; the o- is politeness, but it also keeps the two apart.) If you do want it black: burakku de ii yo.
4. **C**. yaku covers bake, toast and grill, so the moment decides the English. Line 3, hajimete pan o yaite mita, is baking: line 4 says it came out huge (sugoku ookii no ga dekichatte), a whole loaf. Line 6, kitara sugu sono pan o yaite, is toasting: sono pan is the loaf he already has, done the moment Kenta walks in. A misses the loaf; an enormous result only makes sense for baking. B misses sono pan: he already has more bread than he can eat, and a loaf can't be baked sugu.
5. **A**. Line 10: kyou wa asagohan, nuicha dame desu yo. nuicha is from nuku (pull out, leave out), and he used it twice before: itsumo nuiteru (line 2) and asagohan o nuku no wa, karada ni yokunai (line 5). B, tobasu, is where English 'skip' leads, but it's for skipping pages, questions or steps, not meals. C, yasumu, is taking time off from something you go to (shigoto o yasumu, gakkou o yasumu); a meal isn't something you take a day off from.

| # | Speaker | Romaji | English |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Satou-san | moshimoshi, kenta-kun? tonari no satou desu. asa hayaku ni sumimasen ne. | Hello, Kenta? It's Satou from next door. Sorry to call so early. |
| 2 | Satou-san | kinou, kenta-kun, asagohan wa itsumo nuiteru tte ittemashita yo ne. | Yesterday you told me you always skip breakfast, didn't you? |
| 3 | Satou-san | jitsu wa watashi, kinou hajimete pan o yaite mita n desu. | Well, actually, yesterday I tried baking bread for the first time. |
| 4 | Satou-san | sore ga ne, sugoku ookii no ga dekichatte. hitori ja zenbu wa taberarenai n desu yo. | The thing is, it came out enormous. I can't eat it all by myself. |
| 5 | Satou-san | dakara, shigoto ni iku mae ni, uchi ni yotte ikimasen ka? asagohan o nuku no wa, karada ni yokunai desu kara ne. | So why not drop by my place before you go to work? Skipping breakfast isn't good for you, you know. |
| 6 | Satou-san | kenta-kun ga kitara, sugu sono pan o yaite, ocha mo iremasu yo. | As soon as you get here, I'll toast some of that bread and make tea, too. |
| 7 | Satou-san | a, sou ieba, kenta-kun wa koohii ni osatou o mittsu mo iremasu yo ne. | Oh, come to think of it, you put three sugars in your coffee, don't you? |
| 8 | Satou-san | zannen desu kedo, uchi ni wa koohii ga nai n desu. sono kawari, oishii ocha o iremasu kara. | I'm sorry to say I don't have any coffee. But I'll make you a nice cup of green tea instead. |
| 9 | Satou-san | demo, ocha ni wa osatou, irenaide kudasai ne. | Just don't put sugar in the tea, all right? |
| 10 | Satou-san | jaa, oyu o wakashite mattemasu ne. kyou wa asagohan, nuicha dame desu yo. | Well then, I'll put the kettle on and wait for you. No skipping breakfast today! |

</details>

## 71. Whose washing is it?

*Housework and laundry · Casual dialogue · 17 lines · Yuki, Kenta*

Saturday, late morning, at the flat Kenta shares with Sora. Yuki has come round so they can go to a movie together, but Kenta is still in the middle of the housework.

**Collocations:** *kaji o suru* (do the housework); *sentakumono o hosu* (hang out the laundry); *sentakumono ga kawaku* (the laundry dries); *fuku o tatamu* (fold clothes); *soujiki o kakeru* (vacuum (the floor)).

**Review:** *tenki ga ii* (the weather is nice); *ame ga furu* (it rains; rain falls); *heya ga chirakaru* ((a room) gets messy, is a mess).

### Audio script

| Speaker | Voice |
|---|---|
| Yuki | female, late 20s, bright and friendly |
| Kenta | male, late 20s, relaxed and easygoing |

| # | Speaker | Japanese | Kana (if the voice misreads a kanji) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Yuki | ケンタ、来たよ。もう行ける？ | けんた、きたよ。もういける？ |
| 2 | Kenta | ごめん、まだ。朝からずっと家事してるんだ。ソラ、今日仕事だから。 | ごめん、まだ。あさからずっとかじしてるんだ。そら、きょうしごとだから。 |
| 3 | Yuki | そっか、大変だね。じゃあ、手伝うよ。あと何やるの？ | そっか、たいへんだね。じゃあ、てつだうよ。あとなにやるの？ |
| 4 | Kenta | ほんと？洗濯物はもう干したから、あとは掃除機かけるだけ。 | ほんと？せんたくものはもうほしたから、あとはそうじきかけるだけ。 |
| 5 | Yuki | 今日は天気いいから、洗濯物すぐ乾くよ。 | きょうはてんきいいから、せんたくものすぐかわくよ。 |
| 6 | Kenta | うん。じゃあ、掃除機かけちゃうね。あとで、服畳むの手伝って。 | うん。じゃあ、そうじきかけちゃうね。あとで、ふくたたむのてつだって。 |
| 7 | Yuki | あれ？急に雨降ってきた！ケンタ！ | あれ？きゅうにあめふってきた！けんた！ |
| 8 | Kenta | え？何？聞こえない！ | え？なに？きこえない！ |
| 9 | Yuki | 掃除機止めて！雨だよ！洗濯物、早く中に入れなきゃ！ | そうじきとめて！あめだよ！せんたくもの、はやくなかにいれなきゃ！ |
| 10 | Kenta | よかった、間に合った。でも洗濯物、まだ乾いてないのもあるな。 | よかった、まにあった。でもせんたくもの、まだかわいてないのもあるな。 |
| 11 | Yuki | じゃあ、乾いてない洗濯物は部屋に干して。私は服畳むね。 | じゃあ、かわいてないせんたくものはへやにほして。わたしはふくたたむね。 |
| 12 | Kenta | ありがとう。あ、ソラの服は畳まなくていいよ。どうせ部屋散らかってるし。 | ありがとう。あ、そらのふくはたたまなくていいよ。どうせへやちらかってるし。 |
| 13 | Yuki | ソラの服？ねえ、これ、全部ケンタのじゃない？ | そらのふく？ねえ、これ、ぜんぶけんたのじゃない？ |
| 14 | Kenta | え？あ、ほんとだ。そういえばソラ、自分のは自分で洗ってるんだった。 | え？あ、ほんとだ。そういえばそら、じぶんのはじぶんであらってるんだった。 |
| 15 | Yuki | なんだ。ソラもちゃんと家事してるじゃん。 | なんだ。そらもちゃんとかじしてるじゃん。 |
| 16 | Kenta | そうだな。全部一人で家事してると思ってた。 | そうだな。ぜんぶひとりでかじしてるとおもってた。 |
| 17 | Yuki | ほら、早く掃除機かけて。映画、始まっちゃうよ。 | ほら、はやくそうじきかけて。えいが、はじまっちゃうよ。 |

### Questions

1. **What happens in this conversation?**  
   nani ga arimashita ka? (何がありましたか？)
   - A. The rain soaks the washing before they can get it in, so they call off the movie.
   - B. Yuki folds Sora's clothes for him, and Kenta says Sora never does any housework.
   - C. Kenta thought he was doing all the housework, but the washing is all his own: Sora washes his own clothes.

2. **On Monday, Mai asks you about your weekend: "shuumatsu, nani shiteta no?" Say you spent it doing housework.** *(say it)*  
   shuumatsu, nani shiteta no? (週末、何してたの？)

3. **Yuki asks what's left to do, and Kenta tells her about the washing. What did he say?**  
   kenta-san wa sentakumono ni tsuite nan to iimashita ka? (ケンタさんは洗濯物について何と言いましたか？)
   - A. *sentakumono wa mou kaketa kara, ato wa soujiki kakeru dake.* (I've already hung the washing up on hangers, so all that's left is vacuuming.)
   - B. *sentakumono wa mou hoshita kara, ato wa soujiki kakeru dake.* (I've already hung the washing out to dry, so all that's left is vacuuming.)
   - C. *sentakumono wa mou kawaita kara, ato wa soujiki kakeru dake.* (The washing's already dry, so all that's left is vacuuming.)

4. **After they rush the washing inside, Kenta says, "demo sentakumono, mada kawaitenai no mo aru na." What's the problem?**  
   sentakumono wa, dou deshita ka? (洗濯物は、どうでしたか？)
   - A. Some of it still wasn't dry.
   - B. Some of it had got soaked in the rain.
   - C. Some of it hadn't been hung out yet.

5. **Kenta has brought in the dry washing and asks, "sentakumono, zenbu kawaita yo. chotto tetsudatte kureru?" Say you'll fold the clothes.** *(say it)*  
   sentakumono, zenbu kawaita yo. chotto tetsudatte kureru? (洗濯物、全部乾いたよ。ちょっと手伝ってくれる？)

6. **Kenta says, "ato de minna kuru n da kedo, heya, chotto kirei ni shitoite kureru?" Offer to vacuum.** *(say it)*  
   ato de minna kuru n da kedo, heya, chotto kirei ni shitoite kureru? (あとでみんな来るんだけど、部屋、ちょっときれいにしといてくれる？)

<details>
<summary>Answers and transcript</summary>

1. **C**. Line 2: Kenta says he's been doing housework all morning because Sora is at work. Line 12: he tells Yuki not to bother folding Sora's clothes, but in line 13 she points out that everything is his (zenbu kenta no ja nai?). Line 14: he remembers that Sora washes his own clothes himself, and in lines 15-16 Yuki teases him and he admits he thought he was doing all the housework alone. A is wrong: the rain does start (line 7), but they get the washing in in time (line 10: yokatta, maniatta), and in line 17 Yuki is still hurrying him so they don't miss the movie. B is wrong: there are no clothes of Sora's to fold (line 13), and the scene ends with Kenta finding out that Sora does do his own share (lines 14-16).
2. Say you were doing the housework: kaji (o) suru, in the -teta form for 'was doing' (kaji shiteta). You can add when or how long: doyoubi wa (on Saturday), ichinichi (all day), zutto uchi de (at home the whole time). For example: *doyoubi wa ichinichi kaji shiteta yo.* (I spent all of Saturday doing housework.); *zutto uchi de kaji shiteta. tsukareta yo.* (I was home doing housework the whole time. I'm worn out.). Lines 2, 15 and 16: kaji covers all the housework (cooking, cleaning, laundry), and it takes suru, like souji or sentaku. Kenta says asa kara zutto kaji shiteru (I've been doing housework all morning), and later admits he thought he was doing it all alone (hitori de kaji shiteru to omotteta). Don't translate 'housework' word for word as uchi no shigoto: say kaji. Mai is a friend asking casually, so drop o (kaji shiteta); kaji yatteta is just as natural.
3. **B**. Line 4: Kenta says sentakumono wa mou hoshita: hosu is putting the washing out to dry, and he had done that already. A is English 'hang' carried over: kakeru hangs a coat on a hook or a shirt on a hanger, but washing put out to dry is always hosu. C swaps in the partner verb: kawaku is the washing drying by itself, and it wasn't dry yet. In line 5 Yuki says it will dry soon (sugu kawaku yo), and in line 10 some of it still isn't (mada kawaitenai).
4. **A**. Line 10: kawaitenai is kawaku (the washing drying by itself) in the negative -teru form, and mada makes it 'still not dry'. Yuki had expected it to dry quickly in the sun (line 5: sugu kawaku yo), but the sudden rain brought it in early, so in line 11 she says to hang the damp things up indoors. B is wrong: Kenta's yokatta, maniatta (phew, we made it) means they got it in before the rain reached it; still damp from the wash is not the same as soaked by rain. C mixes kawaku up with hosu: hanging it out is hosu, and Kenta had done that already (line 4: mou hoshita).
5. Offer to fold the clothes: fuku (o) tatamu. Casually, watashi ga fuku tatamu ne (I'll fold the clothes), or fuku tatamu no tetsudau yo (I'll help fold them). For example: *ii yo. jaa, watashi ga fuku tatamu ne.* (Sure. I'll fold the clothes, then.); *un, fuku tatamu no tetsudau yo.* (Yeah, I'll help fold the clothes.). Lines 6, 11 and 12: folding clothes is tatamu. Kenta asks for help with it (fuku tatamu no tetsudatte), Yuki takes it on (watashi wa fuku tatamu ne), and Kenta says she needn't fold Sora's (tatamanakute ii yo). Don't use oru for 'fold' here: oru is folding paper, as in origami, or snapping something (hone o oru, break a bone), so fuku o oru sounds wrong. Futons and umbrellas are tatamu too. Kenta is a friend, so drop o and keep it casual.
6. Offer to run the vacuum: soujiki (o) kakeru. The -toku form fits, since people are coming and you're doing it ahead: soujiki kaketoku ne. For example: *ii yo. jaa, soujiki kaketoku ne.* (Sure. I'll run the vacuum round before they get here.); *un, ima soujiki kakeru yo.* (Okay, I'll vacuum now.). Lines 4, 6 and 17: Kenta still has to vacuum (soujiki kakeru dake), says he'll get it done (soujiki kakechau ne), and Yuki hurries him at the end (hayaku soujiki kakete). English 'vacuum' is a verb, but Japanese has no verb for it: you run the machine with kakeru, the same kakeru as mezamashi o kakeru (set an alarm). Casual soujiki suru is also heard; souji suru is cleaning in general, and soujiki o tsukau (use the vacuum) is correct but not how people usually say it. The -toku in kaketoku means doing it ahead, before everyone arrives.

| # | Speaker | Romaji | English |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Yuki | kenta, kita yo. mou ikeru? | Kenta, I'm here. Ready to go? |
| 2 | Kenta | gomen, mada. asa kara zutto kaji shiteru n da. sora, kyou shigoto da kara. | Sorry, not yet. I've been doing housework all morning. Sora's at work today. |
| 3 | Yuki | sokka, taihen da ne. jaa, tetsudau yo. ato nani yaru no? | Oh, that's a lot. I'll help, then. What's left? |
| 4 | Kenta | honto? sentakumono wa mou hoshita kara, ato wa soujiki kakeru dake. | Really? I've already hung the washing out, so it's just the vacuuming now. |
| 5 | Yuki | kyou wa tenki ii kara, sentakumono sugu kawaku yo. | The weather's nice today, so the washing will dry in no time. |
| 6 | Kenta | un. jaa, soujiki kakechau ne. ato de, fuku tatamu no tetsudatte. | Yeah. Right, I'll get the vacuuming done. Help me fold the clothes after, okay? |
| 7 | Yuki | are? kyuu ni ame futte kita! kenta! | Huh? It's suddenly started raining! Kenta! |
| 8 | Kenta | e? nani? kikoenai! | What? I can't hear you! |
| 9 | Yuki | soujiki tomete! ame da yo! sentakumono, hayaku naka ni irenakya! | Turn the vacuum off! It's raining! We need to get the washing in, quick! |
| 10 | Kenta | yokatta, maniatta. demo sentakumono, mada kawaitenai no mo aru na. | Phew, we made it. But some of the washing still isn't dry. |
| 11 | Yuki | jaa, kawaitenai sentakumono wa heya ni hoshite. watashi wa fuku tatamu ne. | Then hang the damp washing up indoors. I'll fold the clothes. |
| 12 | Kenta | arigatou. a, sora no fuku wa tatamanakute ii yo. douse heya chirakatteru shi. | Thanks. Oh, don't bother folding Sora's clothes. His room's a mess anyway. |
| 13 | Yuki | sora no fuku? nee, kore, zenbu kenta no ja nai? | Sora's clothes? Hey, aren't these all yours? |
| 14 | Kenta | e? a, honto da. sou ieba sora, jibun no wa jibun de aratteru n datta. | Huh? Oh, you're right. Come to think of it, Sora washes his own stuff himself. |
| 15 | Yuki | nan da. sora mo chanto kaji shiteru jan. | Well then. So Sora does do his share of the housework. |
| 16 | Kenta | sou da na. zenbu hitori de kaji shiteru to omotteta. | Yeah, fair enough. I thought I was doing all the housework on my own. |
| 17 | Yuki | hora, hayaku soujiki kakete. eiga, hajimatchau yo. | Come on, hurry up and vacuum. The movie's going to start without us. |

</details>

## 72. Where the sound went

*Power, battery, sound · Casual dialogue · 18 lines · Kenta, Sora*

A weekday evening in the flat Kenta and Sora share. Sora gets home with a dead phone and plugs it in. Kenta has been calling him all afternoon about something that has gone missing.

**Collocations:** *dengen o kiru* (turn off (a phone or device)); *dengen o ireru* (turn on (a phone or device)); *juuden ga kireru* (battery runs out, dies); *oto ga deru* (sound comes out (of a TV, speaker, phone)).

**Review:** *denwa ni deru* (answer the phone); *ongaku o kakeru* (put on music); *oto ga suru* (hear a noise, there's a sound).

### Audio script

| Speaker | Voice |
|---|---|
| Kenta | male, late 20s, relaxed and easygoing |
| Sora | male, mid 20s, light voice, playful |

| # | Speaker | Japanese | Kana (if the voice misreads a kanji) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Kenta | あ、ソラ！やっと帰ってきた。なんで電話出なかったの？何度もしたのに。 | あ、そら！やっとかえってきた。なんででんわでなかったの？なんどもしたのに。 |
| 2 | Sora | ごめん！スマホ、昼ぐらいに充電切れちゃったんだ。 | ごめん！スマホ、ひるぐらいにじゅうでんきれちゃったんだ。 |
| 3 | Kenta | え、そうなの？電源切ってるんだと思った。 | え、そうなの？でんげんきってるんだとおもった。 |
| 4 | Sora | 電源なんか切ってないよ。ほんとに充電切れたんだって。 | でんげんなんかきってないよ。ほんとにじゅうでんきれたんだって。 |
| 5 | Kenta | そっか。ね、僕のスピーカー知らない？それで電話したんだけど。 | そっか。ね、ぼくのスピーカーしらない？それででんわしたんだけど。 |
| 6 | Sora | スピーカー？ううん、知らない。あ、ちょっと充電したから、電源入れてみるね。 | スピーカー？ううん、しらない。あ、ちょっとじゅうでんしたから、でんげんいれてみるね。 |
| 7 | Kenta | どう？ついた？ | どう？ついた？ |
| 8 | Sora | うん、ついた。じゃあ、音楽かけてみよう。 | うん、ついた。じゃあ、おんがくかけてみよう。 |
| 9 | Kenta | あれ？音出てないよ。 | あれ？おとでてないよ。 |
| 10 | Sora | ほんとだ。音出ない。なんで？ | ほんとだ。おとでない。なんで？ |
| 11 | Kenta | 一回電源切ってみたら？それで直ることあるよ。 | いっかいでんげんきってみたら？それでなおることあるよ。 |
| 12 | Sora | わかった。電源切って、もう一回電源入れて。これでどう？ | わかった。でんげんきって、もういっかいでんげんいれて。これでどう？ |
| 13 | Kenta | だめだ。やっぱり音出ないね。 | だめだ。やっぱりおとでないね。 |
| 14 | Sora | うそ！充電切れたときに、壊れちゃったのかな。 | うそ！じゅうでんきれたときに、こわれちゃったのかな。 |
| 15 | Kenta | ちょっと待って。なんか、ソラのカバンから音がするんだけど。 | ちょっとまって。なんか、そらのカバンからおとがするんだけど。 |
| 16 | Sora | え？あ！ケンタのスピーカーだ！音、ここから出てたんだ！ | え？あ！けんたのスピーカーだ！おと、ここからでてたんだ！ |
| 17 | Kenta | 僕のスピーカー！ソラ、今朝勝手に使ったでしょ。電源も入れたままだし。 | ぼくのスピーカー！そら、けさかってにつかったでしょ。でんげんもいれたままだし。 |
| 18 | Sora | あ、そうだった。ごめんごめん。でも、スピーカーの充電、切れてなくてよかったね！ | あ、そうだった。ごめんごめん。でも、スピーカーのじゅうでん、きれてなくてよかったね！ |

### Questions

1. **What happens in this scene?**  
   nani ga arimashita ka? (何がありましたか？)
   - A. Sora had switched his phone off, and when he turned it back on the sound didn't work, so Kenta lent him his speaker.
   - B. Sora's phone broke when its battery died, so from now on he'll play his music through Kenta's speaker.
   - C. Sora's phone made no sound because it was playing through Kenta's missing speaker, left switched on in Sora's bag.

2. **When the phone gives no sound, what does Kenta suggest?**  
   kenta wa nan to iimashita ka? (ケンタは何と言いましたか？)
   - A. *ikkai sumaho keshite mitara?* (Why not try turning the phone off (kesu) for a sec?)
   - B. *ikkai dengen kitte mitara?* (Why not try switching it off for a sec?)
   - C. *ikkai dengen kirete mitara?* (Why not try letting the power go off (kireru) for a sec?)

3. **Your phone died at Sora's place and he put it on charge for you. Now he says, "ne, sumaho no juuden, owatta yo." (Hey, your phone's done charging.) Say you'll try turning it on, or ask him to turn it on.** *(say it)*  
   ne, sumaho no juuden, owatta yo. (ね、スマホの充電、終わったよ。)

4. **Yuki tried to call you last night and asks, "kinou no yoru, nande denwa denakatta no?" (Why didn't you pick up last night?) Your phone had died. Tell her.** *(say it)*  
   kinou no yoru, nande denwa denakatta no? (昨日の夜、なんで電話出なかったの？)

5. **When Sora finds the speaker in his bag, he says "oto, koko kara deteta n da!" What does he realise?**  
   sora wa nani ga wakarimashita ka? (ソラは何がわかりましたか？)
   - A. The music from his phone had been coming out of the speaker the whole time.
   - B. The speaker in his bag had been making a strange noise all day.
   - C. His phone's sound had just started working again.

### New words

- **supiikaa** (スピーカー): speaker (for music)
- **kaban** (カバン): bag
- **kowareru** (壊れる): break, stop working (kowarechatta: has broken)
- **naoru** (直る): get fixed, start working again

<details>
<summary>Answers and transcript</summary>

1. **C**. Line 1: Kenta called Sora over and over, and line 5 says why: to ask about his missing speaker. Lines 2 and 4: Sora's phone died (juuden kireta). Lines 6-13: once it is charged and on, Sora puts music on, but no sound comes out of the phone (oto detenai / oto denai), even after switching it off and on. Lines 15-16: the sound is coming from Sora's bag, where Kenta's speaker is playing the music. Line 17: Sora took it this morning and left it switched on. A is wrong: Sora didn't switch his phone off (line 4: dengen nanka kittenai yo), and nobody lent him the speaker; he took it without asking (line 17). B is wrong: the phone only seemed broken (line 14: kowarechatta no kana); the sound was going to the speaker (line 16).
2. **B**. Line 11: ikkai dengen kitte mitara? Switching a device off yourself is dengen o kiru, with transitive kiru, and Sora does it in line 12 (dengen kitte). A is the English speaker's guess: kesu is for lights and the TV (denki o kesu), and sumaho kesu can sound like turning only the screen off. C uses kireru, the intransitive partner: power or a battery cuts out by itself (juuden kireta, line 2), so it isn't something you can try doing. Sora draws the same line in line 4: dengen nanka kittenai yo (I didn't switch it off), honto ni juuden kireta (it really died).
3. Use dengen with ireru, dropping o in casual speech: dengen irete miru (I'll try turning it on), or dengen irete kureru? to ask him to do it. For example: *arigatou. jaa, dengen irete miru.* (Thanks. I'll try turning it on, then.); *honto? jaa, dengen irete kureru?* (Yeah? Could you switch it on for me, then?). The power goes on with ireru ('put in'), as Sora says in line 6 (dengen irete miru ne) and line 12 (mou ikkai dengen irete). English 'turn on' pulls toward tsukeru, but that's the verb for lights and the TV; for a phone's power, ireru is the usual one. tsuita? (is it on?) in line 7 is what the phone does once you've switched it on. Sora is talking to you casually, so answer casually.
4. Say your phone died, with juuden and kireru in the past: gomen, juuden kirechatta n da. For example: *gomen, sumaho no juuden kirechatta n da.* (Sorry, my phone died.); *a, gomen. juuden ga kireteta n da yo.* (Oh, sorry. My phone was dead.). A dead phone is juuden kireta, 'the charge ran out', as Sora tells Kenta in lines 2 and 4. Don't say sumaho shinda (slang) or dengen kitta: that means you switched it off yourself, which is exactly what Sora denies in line 4 (dengen nanka kittenai yo). kirechatta adds 'unfortunately'; kireteta is 'it was dead at the time'. Yuki is a friend, so casual is right.
5. **A**. Line 16: oto ga deru is sound coming out of a device, and deteta n da means 'so that's where it was coming out'. The phone was playing through Kenta's speaker, which is why no sound came out of the phone itself in lines 9-13 (oto detenai, oto denai). B mixes it up with oto ga suru, Kenta's line 15 (kaban kara oto ga suru = I can hear something from your bag): that's hearing a noise, and the speaker was playing Sora's music, which he only put on in line 8. C is wrong: the phone still had no sound of its own; the music was going to the speaker.

| # | Speaker | Romaji | English |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Kenta | a, sora! yatto kaette kita. nande denwa denakatta no? nando mo shita noni. | Sora! Finally, you're back. Why didn't you pick up? I called you over and over. |
| 2 | Sora | gomen! sumaho, hiru gurai ni juuden kirechatta n da. | Sorry! My phone died around lunchtime. |
| 3 | Kenta | e, sou na no? dengen kitteru n da to omotta. | Oh, really? I thought you'd switched it off. |
| 4 | Sora | dengen nanka kittenai yo. honto ni juuden kireta n da tte. | I didn't switch it off! It really did die, I'm telling you. |
| 5 | Kenta | sokka. ne, boku no supiikaa shiranai? sore de denwa shita n da kedo. | Okay. Hey, have you seen my speaker? That's why I was calling. |
| 6 | Sora | supiikaa? uun, shiranai. a, chotto juuden shita kara, dengen irete miru ne. | Your speaker? No, no idea. Oh, it's charged a bit, so I'll try turning it on. |
| 7 | Kenta | dou? tsuita? | Well? Is it on? |
| 8 | Sora | un, tsuita. jaa, ongaku kakete miyou. | Yep, it's on. Okay, let's try putting some music on. |
| 9 | Kenta | are? oto detenai yo. | Huh? There's no sound coming out. |
| 10 | Sora | honto da. oto denai. nande? | You're right. No sound. Why? |
| 11 | Kenta | ikkai dengen kitte mitara? sore de naoru koto aru yo. | Why not try switching it off for a sec? Sometimes that fixes it. |
| 12 | Sora | wakatta. dengen kitte, mou ikkai dengen irete. kore de dou? | Okay. Off, and back on again. How about now? |
| 13 | Kenta | dame da. yappari oto denai ne. | Nope. Still no sound. |
| 14 | Sora | uso! juuden kireta toki ni, kowarechatta no kana. | No way! Maybe it broke when the battery died. |
| 15 | Kenta | chotto matte. nanka, sora no kaban kara oto ga suru n da kedo. | Hang on. I can hear something coming from your bag. |
| 16 | Sora | e? a! kenta no supiikaa da! oto, koko kara deteta n da! | Huh? Oh! It's your speaker! So that's where the sound was coming out! |
| 17 | Kenta | boku no supiikaa! sora, kesa katte ni tsukatta desho. dengen mo ireta mama da shi. | My speaker! You took it this morning without asking, didn't you? And you left it switched on, too. |
| 18 | Sora | a, sou datta. gomen gomen. demo, supiikaa no juuden, kiretenakute yokatta ne! | Oh, right, I did. Sorry, sorry! But hey, lucky the speaker's battery didn't die! |

## 73. Foggy glasses at lunch

*Masks and glasses · Casual dialogue · 18 lines · Yuki, Mai*

Lunchtime at a small café near the office. Mai has a cold and turns up in a mask, and every breath fogs up her glasses. She sits down across from Yuki while they wait for their food.

**Collocations:** *masuku o suru* (wear a mask); *masuku o hazusu* (take off a mask); *megane o kakeru* (wear / put on glasses); *megane o hazusu* (take off glasses).

**Review:** *kaze o hiku* (catch a cold); *nodo ga itai* (have a sore throat); *kaze ga utsuru* (catch someone's cold).

### Audio script

| Speaker | Voice |
|---|---|
| Yuki | female, late 20s, bright and friendly |
| Mai | female, late 20s, warm, a little shy |

| # | Speaker | Japanese | Kana (if the voice misreads a kanji) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Yuki | マイ、こっちこっち！あれ、マスクしてる。風邪ひいたの？ | まい、こっちこっち！あれ、マスクしてる。かぜひいたの？ |
| 2 | Mai | うん、ちょっと喉が痛くて。でもマスクすると、眼鏡がすぐ曇るんだよね。 | うん、ちょっとのどがいたくて。でもマスクすると、めがねがすぐくもるんだよね。 |
| 3 | Yuki | ああ、それ、眼鏡かけてる人はみんな言うよね。 | ああ、それ、めがねかけてるひとはみんないうよね。 |
| 4 | Mai | ほら、今も。前が全然見えない。 | ほら、いまも。まえがぜんぜんみえない。 |
| 5 | Yuki | じゃあ、マスク外したら？ここ、人少ないし。 | じゃあ、マスクはずしたら？ここ、ひとすくないし。 |
| 6 | Mai | ううん、ユキに風邪うつったら嫌だから、マスクはしとく。 | ううん、ゆきにかぜうつったらいやだから、マスクはしとく。 |
| 7 | Yuki | じゃあ、眼鏡のほうを外したら？ | じゃあ、めがねのほうをはずしたら？ |
| 8 | Mai | 眼鏡外すと何も見えないんだけど、まあ、いいか。外しとく。 | めがねはずすとなにもみえないんだけど、まあ、いいか。はずしとく。 |
| 9 | Yuki | そんなに見えないの？ | そんなにみえないの？ |
| 10 | Mai | うん、全然。あ、あそこ、田中さんじゃない？田中さん、お疲れさまです！ | うん、ぜんぜん。あ、あそこ、たなかさんじゃない？たなかさん、おつかれさまです！ |
| 11 | Yuki | ちょっと、マイ！早く眼鏡かけて！あの人、田中さんじゃないよ。 | ちょっと、まい！はやくめがねかけて！あのひと、たなかさんじゃないよ。 |
| 12 | Mai | え、うそ！眼鏡、眼鏡。あ、ほんとだ。全然違う人だ。 | え、うそ！めがね、めがね。あ、ほんとだ。ぜんぜんちがうひとだ。 |
| 13 | Yuki | あの人、めっちゃびっくりしてたよ。もう眼鏡外しちゃだめ。 | あのひと、めっちゃびっくりしてたよ。もうめがねはずしちゃだめ。 |
| 14 | Mai | 恥ずかしい。でも、マスクしてたら、また曇るよ。 | はずかしい。でも、マスクしてたら、またくもるよ。 |
| 15 | Yuki | あ、ご飯来たよ。ほら、マスク外して。 | あ、ごはんきたよ。ほら、マスクはずして。 |
| 16 | Mai | そうだね。マスク外したら、眼鏡かけても曇らない。やっとユキの顔が見えた！ | そうだね。マスクはずしたら、めがねかけてもくもらない。やっとゆきのかおがみえた！ |
| 17 | Yuki | よかった。この話、あとで田中さんに話そうかな。 | よかった。このはなし、あとでたなかさんにはなそうかな。 |
| 18 | Mai | やめて！絶対だめ！ | やめて！ぜったいだめ！ |

### Questions

1. **What happens in this conversation?**  
   nani ga arimashita ka? (何がありましたか？)
   - A. Mai takes her mask off so her glasses stop fogging up, and then Tanaka-san walks in and sees her.
   - B. Yuki doesn't recognise Mai at first, because Mai is wearing a mask and glasses.
   - C. Mai takes off her fogged-up glasses, and without them she greets a stranger, thinking he's Tanaka-san.

2. **When Yuki first suggests taking the mask off, what does Mai say she'll do with it?**  
   mai-san wa, masuku o dou suru to iimashita ka? (マイさんは、マスクをどうすると言いましたか？)
   - A. *masuku wa kitoku.* (I'll keep wearing the mask (with kiru, as for a shirt).)
   - B. *masuku wa shitoku.* (I'll keep the mask on.)
   - C. *masuku wa mottoku.* (I'll hang on to the mask (keep it with me).)

3. **Yuki holds up her phone and says: "OK, I'm taking the photo. Look this way!" You've still got your mask on. Ask her to wait while you take it off.** *(say it)*  
   jaa, shashin toru yo. kotchi mite! (じゃあ、写真撮るよ。こっち見て！)

4. **Mai complains that her mask fogs up her glasses. Yuki says a certain kind of person always says that. Who does she mean?**  
   yuki-san wa, donna hito ga minna sou iu to iimashita ka? (ユキさんは、どんな人がみんなそう言うと言いましたか？)
   - A. People who wear glasses.
   - B. People who are just putting their glasses on.
   - C. People who keep their glasses hooked on their shirt.

5. **You're having ramen with Kenta. The steam hits his face and he groans: "Ugh, my glasses have fogged up and I can't see a thing." Suggest he take them off while he eats.** *(say it)*  
   aa, megane kumotte, zenzen mienai. (ああ、眼鏡曇って、全然見えない。)

### New words

- **kumoru** (曇る): fog up, mist over (glasses); also cloud over (the sky)
- **mieru** (見える): can see, be visible (mienai = can't see, mieta = could see)

<details>
<summary>Answers and transcript</summary>

1. **C**. Lines 2-8: Mai's mask keeps fogging up her glasses. She won't take the mask off in case Yuki catches her cold (line 6), so she takes her glasses off instead. In line 10 she calls out to 'Tanaka-san', and in lines 11-12 Yuki makes her put her glasses back on: it's someone else entirely. A is wrong because Mai only takes the mask off when the food arrives (lines 15-16), and the real Tanaka-san never turns up. B is wrong because Yuki spots Mai straight away and calls her over (line 1).
2. **B**. Line 6: Mai says masuku wa shitoku (shite oku, leave it on), and Yuki noticed it in line 1 with masuku shiteru. Small things you wear, like a mask or a watch, take suru. A, kiru, is where English 'wear' points, but kiru is for clothes you put your body into, like a shirt or jacket (fuku o kiru), not a mask. C, motte oku, means keeping the mask with you, in your hand or your bag, not on your face.
3. Ask her to wait while you take your mask off, using hazusu: chotto matte, masuku hazusu kara. For example: *a, chotto matte. masuku hazusu kara.* (Oh, hang on. Let me take my mask off.); *matte, masuku hazushite mo ii?* (Wait, is it OK if I take my mask off?). A mask comes off with hazusu, as in Yuki's masuku hazushitara? (line 5) and masuku hazushite (line 15). Don't say nugu: that's for clothes and shoes. Casual toru works too (masuku toru kara), but masuku totte on its own can sound like 'pass me a mask'. Putting it back on afterwards is suru again: mata masuku suru.
4. **A**. Line 3: megane kaketeru hito. kakeru is the verb for glasses, and kaketeru describes a state, so this simply means people who wear glasses. It's a way of describing someone. B reads -teru as an action happening right now, but Yuki means glasses-wearers in general, who all complain about masks fogging their lenses. C: kakeru does literally mean 'hang', which is why it goes with glasses (they hang on your ears), but megane kaketeru means they're on your face, not hooked on your clothes.
5. Suggest he take them off with hazusu, the same verb as for a mask: jaa, megane hazushitara? For example: *jaa, megane hazushitara?* (Then why don't you take your glasses off?); *taberu toki wa, megane hazushita hou ga ii yo.* (You'd better take your glasses off while you eat.). Yuki does exactly this in line 7 (megane no hou o hazushitara?), and Mai answers megane hazusu to nanimo mienai (line 8). Glasses and masks share hazusu for taking off, but not for putting on: a mask takes suru, glasses take kakeru (line 16, megane kakete mo kumoranai). Not megane o nugu: nugu is for things you're dressed in, like clothes and shoes.

| # | Speaker | Romaji | English |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Yuki | mai, kotchi kotchi! are, masuku shiteru. kaze hiita no? | Mai, over here! Oh, you're wearing a mask. Have you caught a cold? |
| 2 | Mai | un, chotto nodo ga itakute. demo masuku suru to, megane ga sugu kumoru n da yo ne. | Yeah, my throat's a bit sore. But whenever I wear a mask, my glasses fog up right away. |
| 3 | Yuki | aa, sore, megane kaketeru hito wa minna iu yo ne. | Oh, everyone who wears glasses says that. |
| 4 | Mai | hora, ima mo. mae ga zenzen mienai. | See, it's happening right now. I can't see a thing. |
| 5 | Yuki | jaa, masuku hazushitara? koko, hito sukunai shi. | Then why not take your mask off? There aren't many people in here. |
| 6 | Mai | uun, yuki ni kaze utsuttara iya da kara, masuku wa shitoku. | No, I'd hate for you to catch my cold, so I'll keep it on. |
| 7 | Yuki | jaa, megane no hou o hazushitara? | Then how about taking your glasses off instead? |
| 8 | Mai | megane hazusu to nanimo mienai n da kedo, maa, ii ka. hazushitoku. | I can't see a thing without my glasses, but oh well. I'll take them off for now. |
| 9 | Yuki | sonna ni mienai no? | Is it really that bad? |
| 10 | Mai | un, zenzen. a, asoko, tanaka-san ja nai? tanaka-san, otsukaresama desu! | Yeah, totally. Oh, isn't that Tanaka-san over there? Hi, Tanaka-san! |
| 11 | Yuki | chotto, mai! hayaku megane kakete! ano hito, tanaka-san ja nai yo. | Mai, wait! Put your glasses on, quick! That's not Tanaka-san. |
| 12 | Mai | e, uso! megane, megane. a, honto da. zenzen chigau hito da. | What, no way! Glasses, where are my glasses? Oh, you're right. That's someone else entirely. |
| 13 | Yuki | ano hito, metcha bikkuri shiteta yo. mou megane hazushicha dame. | He looked so startled. No more taking your glasses off. |
| 14 | Mai | hazukashii. demo, masuku shitetara, mata kumoru yo. | So embarrassing. But as long as I've got the mask on, they'll just fog up again. |
| 15 | Yuki | a, gohan kita yo. hora, masuku hazushite. | Oh, the food's here. Come on, take your mask off. |
| 16 | Mai | sou da ne. masuku hazushitara, megane kakete mo kumoranai. yatto yuki no kao ga mieta! | True. With the mask off, my glasses don't fog up. At last I can see your face! |
| 17 | Yuki | yokatta. kono hanashi, ato de tanaka-san ni hanasou kana. | Good. Maybe I'll tell Tanaka-san this story later. |
| 18 | Mai | yamete! zettai dame! | Don't you dare! Absolutely not! |

</details>

## 74. Results at noon

*Exams and results · Polite dialogue · 17 lines · Tanaka-san, Mai*

Just before noon at the office. Last month Mai (Kimura-san at work) sat a bookkeeping exam, and the results go up online at twelve today. Her manager, Tanaka-san, stops by her desk to ask how she's feeling.

**Collocations:** *shiken o ukeru* (take an exam); *ten o toru* (score, get marks (on a test)); *kekka ga deru* (the results come out); *shiken ni ukaru* (pass an exam); *shiken ni ochiru* (fail an exam).

**Review:** *jishin ga aru* (be confident (in yourself, your answer)); *mondai o toku* (solve a question, do problems).

### Audio script

| Speaker | Voice |
|---|---|
| Tanaka-san | male, 40s, calm and kind, polite office speech |
| Mai | female, late 20s, warm, a little shy |

| # | Speaker | Japanese | Kana (if the voice misreads a kanji) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Tanaka-san | 木村さん、先月、簿記の試験を受けましたよね。結果はもう出ましたか？ | きむらさん、せんげつ、ぼきのしけんをうけましたよね。けっかはもうでましたか？ |
| 2 | Mai | まだです。今日の十二時に、結果が出るんです。 | まだです。きょうのじゅうにじに、けっかがでるんです。 |
| 3 | Tanaka-san | もうすぐですね。自信はありますか？ | もうすぐですね。じしんはありますか？ |
| 4 | Mai | いいえ、全然。最後の問題が、ほとんど解けなかったんです。 | いいえ、ぜんぜん。さいごのもんだいが、ほとんどとけなかったんです。 |
| 5 | Tanaka-san | 大丈夫ですよ。あの試験は、七十点取れば受かりますから。 | だいじょうぶですよ。あのしけんは、ななじゅってんとればうかりますから。 |
| 6 | Mai | でも、七十点も取れてないかもしれません。試験に落ちたら、どうしよう。 | でも、ななじゅってんもとれてないかもしれません。しけんにおちたら、どうしよう。 |
| 7 | Tanaka-san | 実は私も、昔あの試験に二回落ちたんですよ。 | じつはわたしも、むかしあのしけんににかいおちたんですよ。 |
| 8 | Mai | え、田中さんがですか？ | え、たなかさんがですか？ |
| 9 | Tanaka-san | はい。試験を三回受けて、やっと受かったんです。 | はい。しけんをさんかいうけて、やっとうかったんです。 |
| 10 | Mai | そうなんですか。じゃあ、もし試験に落ちても、また受ければいいんですね。 | そうなんですか。じゃあ、もししけんにおちても、またうければいいんですね。 |
| 11 | Tanaka-san | そうです。ほら、もう十二時ですよ。 | そうです。ほら、もうじゅうにじですよ。 |
| 12 | Mai | あ、結果出ました！七十二点取れました！ | あ、けっかでました！ななじゅうにてんとれました！ |
| 13 | Tanaka-san | おめでとうございます！一回で試験に受かったんですから、すごいですよ。 | おめでとうございます！いっかいでしけんにうかったんですから、すごいですよ。 |
| 14 | Mai | ありがとうございます！田中さんは、受かったとき、何点取ったんですか？ | ありがとうございます！たなかさんは、うかったとき、なんてんとったんですか？ |
| 15 | Tanaka-san | 九十八点です。同じ試験を三回も受ければ、問題も覚えちゃいますよ。 | きゅうじゅうはちてんです。おなじしけんをさんかいもうければ、もんだいもおぼえちゃいますよ。 |
| 16 | Mai | え、九十八点！じゃあ、来年はもっと難しい試験を受けてみます。 | え、きゅうじゅうはちてん！じゃあ、らいねんはもっとむずかしいしけんをうけてみます。 |
| 17 | Tanaka-san | いいですね。もし落ちても、三回までは大丈夫ですよ。 | いいですね。もしおちても、さんかいまではだいじょうぶですよ。 |

### Questions

1. **What happens in this conversation?**  
   nani ga arimashita ka? (何がありましたか？)
   - A. Mai finds out she failed by two points, and Tanaka-san tells her she can just take it again.
   - B. Mai is worried she's failed, but when the results come out at twelve, she has passed on her first try.
   - C. Tanaka-san got ninety-eight on his first try, so he gives Mai tips before her exam.

2. **At the start, how does Tanaka-san bring up the exam Mai sat last month?**  
   tanaka-san wa, sengetsu no shiken no koto o nan to iimashita ka? (田中さんは、先月の試験のことを何と言いましたか？)
   - A. *sengetsu, boki no shiken ni ukarimashita yo ne.* (You passed the bookkeeping exam last month, didn't you?)
   - B. *sengetsu, boki no shiken o torimashita yo ne.* (You 'took' the bookkeeping exam last month (toru, like English 'take'), didn't you?)
   - C. *sengetsu, boki no shiken o ukemashita yo ne.* (You took (sat) the bookkeeping exam last month, didn't you?)

3. **Sora asks how your Japanese test went: "nihongo no shiken, nanten datta?" You got 85. Tell him your score.** *(say it)*  
   nihongo no shiken, nanten datta? (日本語の試験、何点だった？)

4. **Mai tells Tanaka-san, "kyou no juuniji ni, kekka ga deru n desu." What happens at twelve?**  
   juuniji ni, nani ga arimasu ka? (十二時に、何がありますか？)
   - A. Her exam results are announced.
   - B. She has to go out to collect her results.
   - C. Her exam finishes.

5. **Tanaka-san asks about an exam you took last month. You checked this morning: you passed. Tell him.** *(say it)*  
   sengetsu no shiken no kekka wa, mou demashita ka? (先月の試験の結果は、もう出ましたか？)

6. **You've just found out you failed an exam you took last month. Kenta sees you looking down and asks what's wrong. Tell him.** *(say it)*  
   dou shita no? nanka genki nai ne. (どうしたの？なんか元気ないね。)

### New words

- **boki** (簿記): bookkeeping (a common work qualification exam)
- **-kai** (〜回): times (counter): nikai twice, sankai three times; ikkai de on the first try

<details>
<summary>Answers and transcript</summary>

1. **B**. Lines 3-6: Mai has no confidence and fears she didn't get the seventy points she needs to pass. Line 12: the results come out and she got 72, and in line 13 Tanaka-san congratulates her on passing first time (ikkai de). A is wrong: 72 is two points over the pass mark, not under, and the 'just take it again' idea (line 10) comes before the results. C is wrong: Tanaka-san failed twice (line 7) and passed on his third try (line 9); the 98 was that third time (lines 14-15). Mai's exam was last month (line 1), so it's already over.
2. **C**. Line 1: 'boki no shiken o ukemashita yo ne'. You sit an exam with ukeru and o, and Tanaka-san says it again in line 9 (shiken o sankai ukete) and line 15 (onaji shiken o sankai mo ukereba). B follows English 'take', but toru goes with the score, not the exam: nanajutten toreba (line 5), nanajuuniten toremashita (line 12). A sounds very close, but ukaru (with ni) means pass, and at this point nobody knows the result yet. He asks whether the results are out in the same line, and Mai only finds out she passed in lines 12-13.
3. Give the number with ten fused on, then toru: hachijuugoten totta. Casual speech drops o. Don't use morau (that's a mark someone hands you), and never say shiken o totta: the exam is ukeru, the score is toru. For example: *hachijuugoten totta yo!* (I got eighty-five!); *hachijuugoten. kekkou ii ten toreta yo.* (Eighty-five. I managed a pretty good score.). Score + toru is how Tanaka-san and Mai talk about marks: nanajutten toreba (line 5), nanajuuniten toremashita (line 12), nanten totta n desu ka (line 14). The toreta / toremashita form adds 'managed to get', as in Mai's line 12. hachijuugoten moratta would sound like a teacher just gave you the mark, and shiken o totta mixes up the exam (ukeru) with the score (toru). Sora asks casually, so answer casually.
4. **A**. Line 2: kekka ga deru means the results come out, with the results as the subject, not Mai. At twelve (lines 11-12) she checks and says kekka demashita: they're up, and she got 72. B takes deru in its 'go out, leave' sense, but it's the results that come out, and Mai never leaves her desk. C is wrong because she sat the exam last month (line 1); only the results were still to come.
5. Say the results are out and you passed: shiken ni ukarimashita. The exam takes ni, and the verb is ukaru (ukarimashita), not ukeru (ukemashita), which would only say you sat it. goukaku shimashita also works. For example: *hai, demashita. shiken ni ukarimashita!* (Yes, they're out. I passed!); *hai, kesa demashita. ikkai de shiken ni ukarimashita!* (Yes, they came out this morning. I passed on my first try!). Tanaka-san says it this way in line 13 (ikkai de shiken ni ukatta) and line 9 (yatto ukatta). ukaru and ukeru are one sound apart, and line 9 has both: shiken o sankai ukete, yatto ukatta (took it three times, finally passed). shiken o ukarimashita is wrong: the exam takes ni. Tanaka-san asks politely, so answer with -masu.
6. Say you failed with shiken ni ochiru, casually shiken ochichatta (-chatta adds 'sadly'). English 'fail' tempts shippai shita, but that means you messed something up; for a pass/fail result you 'fall': ochita. For example: *jitsu wa, shiken ochichatta n da.* (Actually, I failed my exam.); *sengetsu no shiken, ochichatta. demo, mata ukeru yo.* (I failed last month's exam. But I'll take it again.). Mai and Tanaka-san use ochiru with ni for failing: shiken ni ochitara (line 6), ano shiken ni nikai ochita (line 7), shiken ni ochite mo (line 10). With a friend, ni usually drops and -chatta adds 'unfortunately'. shiken ni shippai shita means you blew it, which isn't the same as the result. Like Mai in line 10, you can add that you'll just take it again (mata ukeru), with ukeru, not ukaru.

| # | Speaker | Romaji | English |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Tanaka-san | kimura-san, sengetsu, boki no shiken o ukemashita yo ne. kekka wa mou demashita ka? | Kimura-san, you took the bookkeeping exam last month, didn't you? Are the results out yet? |
| 2 | Mai | mada desu. kyou no juuniji ni, kekka ga deru n desu. | Not yet. They come out today at twelve. |
| 3 | Tanaka-san | mou sugu desu ne. jishin wa arimasu ka? | That's any minute now. Are you feeling confident? |
| 4 | Mai | iie, zenzen. saigo no mondai ga, hotondo tokenakatta n desu. | No, not at all. I barely got anywhere with the last question. |
| 5 | Tanaka-san | daijoubu desu yo. ano shiken wa, nanajutten toreba ukarimasu kara. | You'll be fine. On that exam, you pass if you get seventy. |
| 6 | Mai | demo, nanajutten mo toretenai kamo shiremasen. shiken ni ochitara, dou shiyou. | But I might not even have got seventy. What'll I do if I've failed? |
| 7 | Tanaka-san | jitsu wa watashi mo, mukashi ano shiken ni nikai ochita n desu yo. | Actually, I failed that exam twice myself, years ago. |
| 8 | Mai | e, tanaka-san ga desu ka? | What, you did? |
| 9 | Tanaka-san | hai. shiken o sankai ukete, yatto ukatta n desu. | Yes. I took it three times before I finally passed. |
| 10 | Mai | sou na n desu ka. jaa, moshi shiken ni ochite mo, mata ukereba ii n desu ne. | Really? So even if I fail, I can just take it again. |
| 11 | Tanaka-san | sou desu. hora, mou juuniji desu yo. | Exactly. Look, it's twelve already. |
| 12 | Mai | a, kekka demashita! nanajuuniten toremashita! | Oh, the results are up! I got seventy-two! |
| 13 | Tanaka-san | omedetou gozaimasu! ikkai de shiken ni ukatta n desu kara, sugoi desu yo. | Congratulations! You passed on your first try. That's impressive. |
| 14 | Mai | arigatou gozaimasu! tanaka-san wa, ukatta toki, nanten totta n desu ka? | Thank you! What did you get when you passed? |
| 15 | Tanaka-san | kyuujuuhachiten desu. onaji shiken o sankai mo ukereba, mondai mo oboechaimasu yo. | Ninety-eight. Take the same exam three times and you end up knowing the questions. |
| 16 | Mai | e, kyuujuuhachiten! jaa, rainen wa motto muzukashii shiken o ukete mimasu. | Ninety-eight! Then next year I'll try a harder exam. |
| 17 | Tanaka-san | ii desu ne. moshi ochite mo, sankai made wa daijoubu desu yo. | Good idea. And if you fail, you're fine up to three tries. |

</details>

## 75. Two minutes late

*Out on errands · Casual monologue · 12 lines · Okaasan*

Saturday, late afternoon. It's Kenji's birthday, and Okaasan has gone out to buy his cake at the family's usual cake shop, which only takes cash. Yuki doesn't pick up, so Okaasan leaves her three voicemails: one from the queue at the bank by the station, one once she has her cash, and one outside the cake shop.

**Collocations:** *okane o orosu* (withdraw cash); *mise ga aku* (a shop opens (aiteru = is open)); *mise ga shimaru* (a shop closes (shimatteru = is closed)); *junban o matsu* (wait your turn).

**Review:** *kaado o tsukau* (pay by card); *hito ga ooi* (be crowded, lots of people); *jikan ga aku* (be free (have time)).

### Audio script

| Speaker | Voice |
|---|---|
| Okaasan | female, 50s, warm, motherly |

| # | Speaker | Japanese | Kana (if the voice misreads a kanji) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Okaasan | ユキ？お母さんだけど。今ね、駅の前の銀行に、お金下ろしに来てるの。 | ゆき？おかあさんだけど。いまね、えきのまえのぎんこうに、おかねおろしにきてるの。 |
| 2 | Okaasan | ケンジの誕生日のケーキ、いつもの店で買うんだけど、あそこ、カード使えないのよ。 | けんじのたんじょうびのけーき、いつものみせでかうんだけど、あそこ、かーどつかえないのよ。 |
| 3 | Okaasan | でも、土曜日だからか、人が多くてね。もう二十分も順番待ってるの。 | でも、どようびだからか、ひとがおおくてね。もうにじゅっぷんもじゅんばんまってるの。 |
| 4 | Okaasan | まあ、店は六時まで開いてるから、閉まる前には行けると思うけど。 | まあ、みせはろくじまであいてるから、しまるまえにはいけるとおもうけど。 |
| 5 | Okaasan | ユキ、またお母さん。やっとお金下ろせたわ。結局三十分よ。 | ゆき、またおかあさん。やっとおかねおろせたわ。けっきょくさんじゅっぷんよ。 |
| 6 | Okaasan | 前の小さい子どもがね、ずっと「まだ？まだ？」って言ってて。 | まえのちいさいこどもがね、ずっと「まだ？まだ？」っていってて。 |
| 7 | Okaasan | 「ちゃんと順番待ってね」って言われてたわ。ケンジも昔はそうだったわね。 | 「ちゃんとじゅんばんまってね」っていわれてたわ。けんじもむかしはそうだったわね。 |
| 8 | Okaasan | あ、もう六時十分前！早く行かないと、店閉まっちゃう。じゃあね。 | あ、もうろくじじゅっぷんまえ！はやくいかないと、みせしまっちゃう。じゃあね。 |
| 9 | Okaasan | ユキ、何度もごめんね。店、もう閉まってたの。 | ゆき、なんどもごめんね。みせ、もうしまってたの。 |
| 10 | Okaasan | 二分遅かったのよ。せっかくお金下ろしたのに。 | にふんおそかったのよ。せっかくおかねおろしたのに。 |
| 11 | Okaasan | それでお願いなんだけど、帰りにケーキ買ってきてくれない？時間空いてたらでいいから。 | それでおねがいなんだけど、かえりにけーきかってきてくれない？じかんあいてたらでいいから。 |
| 12 | Okaasan | 駅の中の店なら、八時まで開いてるから。お金はあとであげるね。 | えきのなかのみせなら、はちじまであいてるから。おかねはあとであげるね。 |

### Questions

1. **What happened in Okaasan's messages?**  
   nani ga arimashita ka? (何がありましたか？)
   - A. The bank queue was so long that she gave up on getting cash, so she wants Yuki to pay for the cake by card.
   - B. She got her cash, but the cake shop had closed by the time she got there, so she's asking Yuki to pick up a cake on the way home.
   - C. She bought the cake in time, but now she's out of cash, so she's asking Yuki to stop at the bank on the way home.

2. **You're out for dinner with Kenta. When it's time to pay, he says: "Apparently this place doesn't take cards." You have no cash on you, and there's a konbini right by. Tell him you'll go and get some cash.** *(say it)*  
   kono mise, kaado tsukaenai tte. (この店、カード使えないって。)

3. **In her last message, Okaasan says the shop inside the station is "hachiji made aiteru". What does she mean?**  
   okaasan wa, eki no naka no mise ni tsuite nan to iimashita ka? (お母さんは、駅の中の店について何と言いましたか？)
   - A. It opens at eight, so Yuki would have to wait around.
   - B. Yuki is free until eight, so she has time to go.
   - C. It's open until eight, so Yuki can still get there in time.

4. **It's ten at night. Kenta says he's heading out to the shop by the station and asks if you want anything. You know that shop closes at nine. Tell him it's already closed.** *(say it)*  
   ima kara eki no mae no mise ni kaimono ni iku kedo, nanika hoshii mono aru? (今から駅の前の店に買い物に行くけど、何か欲しいものある？)

5. **The little kid in front of Okaasan kept asking "mada?". What was he told?**  
   mae no kodomo wa, nan to iwarete imashita ka? (前の子どもは、何と言われていましたか？)
   - A. *chanto junban matte ne.* (Wait your turn nicely, OK?)
   - B. *chanto anata no ban o matte ne.* (Wait for your (anata no) turn nicely, OK?)
   - C. *chanto junban machigaenaide ne.* (Don't get the order mixed up, OK?)

### New words

- **ginkou** (銀行): bank
- **keeki** (ケーキ): cake

<details>
<summary>Answers and transcript</summary>

1. **B**. Lines 1-3: Okaasan is at the bank getting cash for Kenji's birthday cake, because the usual shop doesn't take cards, and she has been waiting her turn for twenty minutes. Line 5: she finally gets the cash, and in line 8 she hurries off before the shop closes. Lines 9-10: it was already closed, and she was two minutes late. Lines 11-12: she asks Yuki to buy a cake on her way home at the shop inside the station, which is open till eight. A is wrong because she did get the cash (line 5), and the cake shop doesn't take cards anyway (line 2). C is wrong because she never got the cake (line 9). She has the cash, and the favour is buying the cake.
2. Say you'll go and get cash with okane o orosu plus -te kuru (go, do it, come back): chotto okane oroshite kuru ne. Not okane o toru. For example: *e, honto? jaa, chotto okane oroshite kuru ne.* (Really? Then I'll just go and grab some cash.); *jaa, konbini de okane oroshite kuru kara, chotto mattete.* (Then I'll go get some cash at the konbini. Wait here a sec.). Getting cash from a machine is orosu ('bring down'). Okaasan went to the bank okane oroshi ni (line 1), finally okane oroseta (line 5), and complains sekkaku okane oroshita noni (line 10). Adding -te kuru makes it 'go and do it, then come back', which is what you say as you head off. okane o toru, the word-for-word 'take money', sounds like taking someone's money. Casual okane dasu also works. Kenta's cue is the same problem Okaasan has in line 2 (kaado tsukaenai).
3. **C**. Line 12: eki no naka no mise nara, hachiji made aiteru kara. A shop that aiteru is open, and made says until when, so it's open till eight. Okaasan said the same about the cake shop in line 4 (rokuji made aiteru). A would be hachiji ni aku, because plain aku is the moment a shop opens. B mixes it up with the other aiteru, one line earlier: jikan aitetara de ii kara (line 11) means 'only if you're free'. That one is about Yuki's time, not the shop.
4. Tell him it's shut with shimatteru (is closed): ano mise, mou shimatteru yo. For the closing time, use plain shimaru: kuji ni shimaru. For example: *e, ano mise, mou shimatteru yo.* (Huh? That shop's already closed.); *ano mise, kuji ni shimaru kara, mou shimatteru n ja nai?* (That shop closes at nine, so it's probably shut by now, isn't it?). A shop closing is shimaru. Okaasan says shimaru mae ni (before it closes, line 4), mise shimatchau (the shop'll close, line 8) and mise, mou shimatteta (it was already closed, line 9). For how the shop is right now, use shimatteru. shimeru is what the staff do when they close up, so it fits when you ask them (nanji ni shimemasu ka?). Casual owaru also works: mou owatteru yo.
5. **A**. Line 7: chanto junban matte ne. Waiting your turn is junban o matsu, and in casual speech the o drops. Okaasan uses it about herself in line 3 (mou nijuppun mo junban matteru). Standing in the queue itself would be narabu. B is English 'wait for your turn' word for word: Japanese leaves out 'your' here, and anata sounds cold from a parent to a small child. C sounds close (junban, machi-) but means 'don't get the order wrong'. The kid was impatient, not muddled.

| # | Speaker | Romaji | English |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Okaasan | yuki? okaasan da kedo. ima ne, eki no mae no ginkou ni, okane oroshi ni kiteru no. | Yuki? It's Mum. I'm at the bank by the station, getting some cash out. |
| 2 | Okaasan | kenji no tanjoubi no keeki, itsumo no mise de kau n da kedo, asoko, kaado tsukaenai no yo. | I'm getting Kenji's birthday cake at the usual place, but they don't take cards. |
| 3 | Okaasan | demo, doyoubi da kara ka, hito ga ookute ne. mou nijuppun mo junban matteru no. | But it's packed, maybe because it's Saturday. I've already been waiting my turn for twenty minutes. |
| 4 | Okaasan | maa, mise wa rokuji made aiteru kara, shimaru mae ni wa ikeru to omou kedo. | Well, the shop's open till six, so I should get there before it closes. |
| 5 | Okaasan | yuki, mata okaasan. yatto okane oroseta wa. kekkyoku sanjuppun yo. | Yuki, Mum again. I finally got the cash out. Thirty minutes, in the end! |
| 6 | Okaasan | mae no chiisai kodomo ga ne, zutto mada? mada? tte ittete. | The little kid in front of me kept going, 'Is it our turn yet? Is it our turn yet?' |
| 7 | Okaasan | chanto junban matte ne tte iwareteta wa. kenji mo mukashi wa sou datta wa ne. | He kept getting told, 'Wait your turn nicely, OK?' Kenji was just like that when he was little. |
| 8 | Okaasan | a, mou rokuji juppun mae! hayaku ikanai to, mise shimatchau. jaa ne. | Oh, it's already ten to six! If I don't hurry, the shop'll close. Bye! |
| 9 | Okaasan | yuki, nando mo gomen ne. mise, mou shimatteta no. | Yuki, sorry for all the calls. The shop was already closed. |
| 10 | Okaasan | nifun osokatta no yo. sekkaku okane oroshita noni. | I was two minutes too late. And after all that trouble getting the cash out, too. |
| 11 | Okaasan | sore de onegai na n da kedo, kaeri ni keeki katte kite kurenai? jikan aitetara de ii kara. | So, a favour: could you pick up a cake on your way home? Only if you've got time. |
| 12 | Okaasan | eki no naka no mise nara, hachiji made aiteru kara. okane wa ato de ageru ne. | The shop inside the station is open till eight. I'll give you the money later. |

## 76. A lighter for Dad

*Long-term health · Casual dialogue · 18 lines · Kenji, Yuki*

Saturday afternoon at the family home. Yuki is back for the weekend and their dad's birthday is tomorrow. Kenji, half awake on the sofa, shows her the present he has bought.

**Collocations:** *karada ni ii* (good for you, healthy); *toshi o toru* (get older, age); *tabako o yameru* (quit smoking); *arerugii ga aru* (be allergic (to something)).

**Review:** *kaisha o yameru* (quit one's job); *tabako o suu* (smoke (a cigarette)); *ocha o nomu* (have tea, drink tea).

### Audio script

| Speaker | Voice |
|---|---|
| Kenji | male, late teens, sleepy, mumbling |
| Yuki | female, late 20s, bright and friendly |

| # | Speaker | Japanese | Kana (if the voice misreads a kanji) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Kenji | ねえ、見て。お父さんの誕生日プレゼント、これにした。 | ねえ、みて。おとうさんのたんじょうびぷれぜんと、これにした。 |
| 2 | Yuki | え、ライター？ケンジ、知らないの？お父さん、先月やめたんだよ。 | え、らいたー？けんじ、しらないの？おとうさん、せんげつやめたんだよ。 |
| 3 | Kenji | やめた？え、会社やめたの？ | やめた？え、かいしゃやめたの？ |
| 4 | Yuki | 違うよ！タバコ。タバコやめたの。 | ちがうよ！たばこ。たばこやめたの。 |
| 5 | Kenji | マジで？あんなにタバコ吸ってたのに。なんで？ | まじで？あんなにたばこすってたのに。なんで？ |
| 6 | Yuki | もう年取ったからなって。最近、体にいいことばっかりしてるよ。 | もうとしとったからなって。さいきん、からだにいいことばっかりしてるよ。 |
| 7 | Kenji | ああ、だから朝、コーヒーじゃなくてお茶飲んでるんだ。 | ああ、だからあさ、こーひーじゃなくておちゃのんでるんだ。 |
| 8 | Yuki | そう。お茶は体にいいからって。 | そう。おちゃはからだにいいからって。 |
| 9 | Kenji | タバコやめたんなら、これ、もう使わないよね。どうしよう。 | たばこやめたんなら、これ、もうつかわないよね。どうしよう。 |
| 10 | Yuki | 体にいいものにしたら？年を取ると、そういうのがうれしいらしいよ。 | からだにいいものにしたら？としをとると、そういうのがうれしいらしいよ。 |
| 11 | Kenji | じゃあ、そばは？いいそばとか。 | じゃあ、そばは？いいそばとか。 |
| 12 | Yuki | そばはダメ。お父さん、そばアレルギーあるじゃん。 | そばはだめ。おとうさん、そばあれるぎーあるじゃん。 |
| 13 | Kenji | え、アレルギーあるの？知らなかった。 | え、あれるぎーあるの？しらなかった。 |
| 14 | Yuki | もう、ケンジはお父さんのこと、何も知らないんだから。タバコやめたのも知らなかったし。 | もう、けんじはおとうさんのこと、なにもしらないんだから。たばこやめたのもしらなかったし。 |
| 15 | Kenji | ごめんって。じゃあ、お茶は？お茶なら、アレルギーないよね？ | ごめんって。じゃあ、おちゃは？おちゃなら、あれるぎーないよね？ |
| 16 | Yuki | うん、ない。いいね、それ。今度はちゃんと考えたじゃん。 | うん、ない。いいね、それ。こんどはちゃんとかんがえたじゃん。 |
| 17 | Kenji | ああ、考えたら疲れた。年取ったかな。 | ああ、かんがえたらつかれた。としとったかな。 |
| 18 | Yuki | 十七で何言ってんの。昨日遅くまで起きてただけでしょ。 | じゅうななでなにいってんの。きのうおそくまでおきてただけでしょ。 |

### Questions

1. **What happens in this conversation?**  
   nani ga arimashita ka? (何がありましたか？)
   - A. Kenji finds out his dad has quit his job, so they pick tea to cheer him up.
   - B. Kenji's lighter is no good as a present because his dad has quit smoking, so he picks tea instead.
   - C. Kenji wants to give his dad some good soba, and Yuki agrees because soba is good for you.

2. **You always drink tea, never coffee. Kenji asks you, "nande itsumo ocha na no? koohii no hou ga oishii jan." Tell him tea is good for you, using karada ni ii.** *(say it)*  
   nande itsumo ocha na no? koohii no hou ga oishii jan. (なんでいつもお茶なの？コーヒーのほうがおいしいじゃん。)

3. **At the end, Kenji says all that thinking has worn him out. What does he say next?**  
   kenji wa saigo ni nan to iimashita ka? (ケンジは最後に何と言いましたか？)
   - A. *furuku natta kana* (have I gone old and worn? (said of things))
   - B. *toshi ga ue kana* (am I the older one?)
   - C. *toshi totta kana* (must be getting old)

4. **Kenji says, "tabako yameta n nara, kore, mou tsukawanai yo ne." What does he mean?**  
   kenji wa nani ga iitakatta n desu ka? (ケンジは何が言いたかったんですか？)
   - A. His dad has given up smoking, so he won't have any use for the lighter now.
   - B. His dad smokes less these days, so he'll only use the lighter now and then.
   - C. His dad has left his job, so he won't need anything for the office.

5. **Like Kenji's dad, you're allergic to soba. Yuki asks you, "nee, ohiru, soba ni shinai?" Say you can't have it because of your allergy.** *(say it)*  
   nee, ohiru, soba ni shinai? (ねえ、お昼、そばにしない？)

### New words

- **otousan** (お父さん): dad, father
- **purezento** (プレゼント): present, gift
- **raitaa** (ライター): (cigarette) lighter
- **soba** (そば): soba, buckwheat noodles

<details>
<summary>Answers and transcript</summary>

1. **B**. Line 1: Kenji has bought his dad a lighter for his birthday. Lines 2-4: Yuki tells him their dad quit smoking last month (tabako yameta), and in line 9 Kenji sees the lighter is no use now. Soba is out because of an allergy (line 12), and in lines 15-16 they settle on tea. A is wrong: in line 3 Kenji thinks his dad quit his job (kaisha yameta), but Yuki corrects him straight away in line 4: it's smoking. C is wrong: Kenji does suggest soba (line 11), but Yuki says no, because their dad is allergic to it (line 12).
2. Say tea is good for you: ocha wa karada ni ii kara, keeping the ni even in casual speech. You could add that tea is better for you than coffee (ocha no hou ga karada ni ii). For example: *datte, ocha wa karada ni ii kara.* (Because tea's good for you.); *koohii mo suki da kedo, ocha no hou ga karada ni ii n da yo.* (I like coffee too, but tea's better for you.). In line 8 Yuki explains why their dad drinks tea now: ocha wa karada ni ii kara tte (because tea's good for you, he says), and lines 6 and 10 use karada ni ii for healthy habits and presents. It's said of food, drinks and habits, and ni stays even in casual talk. Don't say ocha wa kenkou desu: kenkou is a person's health, not something that's good for you. Kenji asks casually, so answer casually.
3. **C**. Line 17: toshi totta kana. People take on years, toshi o toru, and 'I'm getting old' comes out in the past (toshi totta), with o dropped in casual talk. Their dad's excuse in line 6 is the same phrase (mou toshi totta kara na), and Yuki uses the general form in line 10 (toshi o toru to, as you get older). A is wrong: furuku naru is for things wearing out, like old shoes, not people. B is wrong: toshi ga ue means older than someone, and Kenji isn't comparing himself with anyone.
4. **A**. Line 9: tabako yameta means he has quit smoking. Yameru is giving up a habit for good, so Kenji adds mou tsukawanai (he won't use it anymore), meaning the lighter is no use as a present. B is wrong: yameta means he stopped completely, not that he cut down. C mixes it up with kaisha o yameru (quit your job), the same-sounding yameru Kenji wrongly jumped to in line 3. Yuki corrects him in line 4 (chigau yo! tabako).
5. Say you have a soba allergy: soba arerugii aru (ga drops in casual talk), or soba no arerugii ga aru. Put the cause in front of arerugii, then suggest something else. For example: *a, gomen. soba arerugii aru n da.* (Oh, sorry. I'm allergic to soba.); *gomen, watashi, soba no arerugii ga aru kara, hoka no ni shinai?* (Sorry, I've got a soba allergy, so can we have something else?). An allergy is something you have, so it takes aru: Yuki says otousan, soba arerugii aru jan (line 12), Kenji echoes arerugii aru no? (line 13), and in line 15 he uses the negative, arerugii nai. The cause goes in front: soba arerugii, or soba no arerugii. Say it a-re-ru-gii, not like English 'allergy'. Yuki asks casually, so answer casually.

| # | Speaker | Romaji | English |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Kenji | nee, mite. otousan no tanjoubi purezento, kore ni shita. | Hey, look. This is what I got Dad for his birthday. |
| 2 | Yuki | e, raitaa? kenji, shiranai no? otousan, sengetsu yameta n da yo. | Huh, a lighter? Kenji, didn't you know? Dad quit last month. |
| 3 | Kenji | yameta? e, kaisha yameta no? | Quit? Wait, he quit his job? |
| 4 | Yuki | chigau yo! tabako. tabako yameta no. | No! Smoking. He quit smoking. |
| 5 | Kenji | maji de? anna ni tabako sutteta noni. nande? | Seriously? After all that smoking? How come? |
| 6 | Yuki | mou toshi totta kara na tte. saikin, karada ni ii koto bakkari shiteru yo. | He says it's because he's getting old. Lately he's all about stuff that's good for him. |
| 7 | Kenji | aa, dakara asa, koohii ja nakute ocha nonderu n da. | Oh, so that's why he has tea in the morning now, not coffee. |
| 8 | Yuki | sou. ocha wa karada ni ii kara tte. | Yep. He says tea's good for you. |
| 9 | Kenji | tabako yameta n nara, kore, mou tsukawanai yo ne. dou shiyou. | If he's quit smoking, he won't be using this anymore, will he? What do I do? |
| 10 | Yuki | karada ni ii mono ni shitara? toshi o toru to, sou iu no ga ureshii rashii yo. | Why not something that's good for him? Apparently that's what you like getting as you get older. |
| 11 | Kenji | jaa, soba wa? ii soba toka. | Then how about soba? Some really good soba or something. |
| 12 | Yuki | soba wa dame. otousan, soba arerugii aru jan. | Not soba. Dad's allergic to soba, remember? |
| 13 | Kenji | e, arerugii aru no? shiranakatta. | Wait, he has an allergy? I didn't know that. |
| 14 | Yuki | mou, kenji wa otousan no koto, nanimo shiranai n da kara. tabako yameta no mo shiranakatta shi. | Honestly, Kenji, you don't know a thing about Dad. You didn't even know he'd quit smoking. |
| 15 | Kenji | gomen tte. jaa, ocha wa? ocha nara, arerugii nai yo ne? | All right, sorry! So what about tea? He's not allergic to tea, is he? |
| 16 | Yuki | un, nai. ii ne, sore. kondo wa chanto kangaeta jan. | Nope, he isn't. That's a good one. You actually thought it through this time. |
| 17 | Kenji | aa, kangaetara tsukareta. toshi totta kana. | Ugh, all that thinking wore me out. Must be getting old. |
| 18 | Yuki | juunana de nani itten no. kinou osoku made okiteta dake desho. | You're seventeen, what are you on about? You just stayed up too late last night. |

## 77. Persimmons from next door

*Motivation and mood · Polite dialogue · 17 lines · Satou-san, Yuki*

Saturday morning outside a small apartment building. Yuki has come down to the mailboxes in her sweatpants, her washing still sitting in the machine upstairs. Next door, Satou-san, the elderly neighbour, is hanging out his laundry beside a basket of persimmons he has just picked from his tree.

**Collocations:** *yaruki ga nai* (have no motivation, can't be bothered); *yaruki ga deru* (get motivated); *genki ga deru* (perk up, get a lift); *tenshon ga agaru* (get excited, get hyped); *sutoresu ga tamaru* (get stressed, stress builds up).

**Review:** *sentakumono ga kawaku* (the laundry dries); *kaji o suru* (do the housework); *sentakumono o hosu* (hang out the laundry).

### Audio script

| Speaker | Voice |
|---|---|
| Satou-san | male, 70s, polite, kind, unhurried |
| Yuki | female, late 20s, bright and friendly |

| # | Speaker | Japanese | Kana (if the voice misreads a kanji) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Satou-san | おはようございます、ユキさん。今日は洗濯物がよく乾きそうですね。 | おはようございます、ゆきさん。きょうはせんたくものがよくかわきそうですね。 |
| 2 | Yuki | おはようございます。そうですね。でも今日はなんか、やる気がないんですよね。 | おはようございます。そうですね。でもきょうはなんか、やるきがないんですよね。 |
| 3 | Satou-san | どうしたんですか？仕事が忙しかったんですか？ | どうしたんですか？しごとがいそがしかったんですか？ |
| 4 | Yuki | はい。今週はずっと忙しくて、ストレスが溜まってるんです。 | はい。こんしゅうはずっといそがしくて、すとれすがたまってるんです。 |
| 5 | Satou-san | それは大変でしたね。ストレスが溜まると、やる気もなくなりますよね。 | それはたいへんでしたね。すとれすがたまると、やるきもなくなりますよね。 |
| 6 | Yuki | 今日は家事をしようと思ってたんですけど、全然やる気が出なくて。 | きょうはかじをしようとおもってたんですけど、ぜんぜんやるきがでなくて。 |
| 7 | Satou-san | やる気はね、始めたら出てくるものですよ。まずはちょっとだけ、やってみたらどうですか？ | やるきはね、はじめたらでてくるものですよ。まずはちょっとだけ、やってみたらどうですか？ |
| 8 | Yuki | 始めたら、ですか。でも、その始めるやる気がないんですよ。 | はじめたら、ですか。でも、そのはじめるやるきがないんですよ。 |
| 9 | Satou-san | じゃあ、うちの柿、どうぞ。食べて、元気出してください。 | じゃあ、うちのかき、どうぞ。たべて、げんきだしてください。 |
| 10 | Yuki | え、柿！大好きなんです。うわあ、テンション上がります！ | え、かき！だいすきなんです。うわあ、てんしょんあがります！ |
| 11 | Satou-san | テンションが上がる、ですか。面白い言い方ですね。 | てんしょんがあがる、ですか。おもしろいいいかたですね。 |
| 12 | Yuki | ありがとうございます。なんかもう、元気が出ました。 | ありがとうございます。なんかもう、げんきがでました。 |
| 13 | Satou-san | それはよかった。ストレスが溜まった時は、おいしいものが一番ですよ。 | それはよかった。すとれすがたまったときは、おいしいものがいちばんですよ。 |
| 14 | Yuki | あ、やる気もちょっと出てきました。まず洗濯物、干してきます！ | あ、やるきもちょっとでてきました。まずせんたくもの、ほしてきます！ |
| 15 | Satou-san | いいですね。ユキさんが元気だと、私も元気が出ますよ。 | いいですね。ゆきさんがげんきだと、わたしもげんきがでますよ。 |
| 16 | Yuki | え、じゃあ佐藤さんも、テンション上がりました？ | え、じゃあさとうさんも、てんしょんあがりました？ |
| 17 | Satou-san | ええ、テンション上がりましたよ。初めて使いました。 | ええ、てんしょんあがりましたよ。はじめてつかいました。 |

### Questions

1. **What happens in this conversation?**  
   nani ga arimashita ka? (何がありましたか？)
   - A. Satou-san offers to hang out Yuki's laundry for her, since she's worn out from work.
   - B. Yuki decides to skip the housework and rest today, as Satou-san suggests.
   - C. Yuki can't face the housework, but Satou-san's persimmons cheer her up and she goes off to hang out her laundry.

2. **It's Sunday and you just want to lie around. Kenta asks you, "nee, kyou nani suru? dokka ikanai?" Tell him you just don't feel like it today, using yaruki ga nai.** *(say it)*  
   nee, kyou nani suru? dokka ikanai? (ねえ、今日何する？どっか行かない？)

3. **Satou-san tells Yuki how motivation works. When does he say it comes?**  
   satou-san wa, yaruki wa itsu deru to iimashita ka? (佐藤さんは、やる気はいつ出ると言いましたか？)
   - A. After a proper rest, so she should take the morning off first.
   - B. Once she gets started, so she should try doing just a little first.
   - C. When she eats something tasty, like his persimmons.

4. **You've had an awful day at work. Mai hands you a hot coffee and says, "hai, kore. muri shinaide ne." Thank her and tell her it's cheered you up, using genki ga deru.** *(say it)*  
   hai, kore. muri shinaide ne. (はい、これ。無理しないでね。)

5. **When Yuki sees the persimmons, what does she say?**  
   kaki o mite, yuki-san wa nan to iimashita ka? (柿を見て、ユキさんは何と言いましたか？)
   - A. *tenshon agarimasu!* (I'm getting so excited!)
   - B. *koufun shimasu!* (I'm getting all worked up!)
   - C. *tenshon sagarimasu!* (What a downer!)

6. **You've been working late every night. Your manager, Tanaka-san, asks you, "saikin, tsukaretemasen ka? daijoubu desu ka?" Tell him honestly that stress has been piling up, using sutoresu ga tamaru.** *(say it)*  
   saikin, tsukaretemasen ka? daijoubu desu ka? (最近、疲れてませんか？大丈夫ですか？)

### New words

- **kaki** (柿): persimmon
- **iikata** (言い方): way of saying something

<details>
<summary>Answers and transcript</summary>

1. **C**. Lines 2-8: after a stressful week, Yuki has no motivation (yaruki ga nai) and can't get going on the housework (yaruki ga denakute). In line 9 Satou-san gives her persimmons from his tree; she gets excited (line 10), feels better (line 12), and in line 14 goes off to hang out her laundry. A is wrong: Satou-san never offers to do her laundry; he tells her to try doing a little herself (line 7). B is wrong: he says motivation comes once you start, not that she should rest, and she doesn't take the day off: she starts with the laundry (line 14).
2. Say you can't be bothered today: kyou wa nanka yaruki nai. With a friend ga can drop, and gomen softens it. For example: *gomen, kyou wa nanka yaruki nai. uchi ni iru yo.* (Sorry, I just can't be bothered today. I'm staying in.); *kyou wa yaruki ga nai n da. mata kondo ne.* (I've got no motivation today. Another time, OK?). In line 2 Yuki tells Satou-san kyou wa nanka, yaruki ga nai n desu yo ne (today I just can't be bothered), and in line 8 she says she doesn't even have the motivation to start (sono hajimeru yaruki ga nai n desu yo). About yourself, yaruki nai is a flat 'I don't feel like it'; about someone else (ano hito yaruki nai) it's a criticism. Avoid yaruki ga arimasen: it sounds stiff, and even politely people say yaruki ga nai n desu, as Yuki does. Kenta is a friend, so answer casually.
3. **B**. In line 7 Satou-san says yaruki wa ne, hajimetara dete kuru mono desu yo: motivation comes out (deru) once you start, so try a little first. Yuki uses the negative in line 6 (zenzen yaruki ga denakute, I just can't get going), and in line 14 it finally comes: yaruki mo chotto dete kimashita. A is wrong: he never tells her to rest; his advice is to start. C mixes up yaruki with genki: the persimmons are to lift her mood (genki dashite, line 9), and good food is his cure for stress (line 13), not where motivation comes from.
4. Thank her and say it gave you a lift, in the past: arigatou, genki deta. It's about you, so it's deta; genki dashite is what you say to cheer someone else up. For example: *arigatou. nanka genki deta.* (Thanks. That's cheered me up.); *uwaa, ureshii. arigatou, sugoku genki deta yo.* (Aw, that's so nice. Thanks, it's really cheered me up.). In line 9 Satou-san hands Yuki persimmons and says tabete, genki dashite kudasai (eat them and cheer up): genki dashite is what you say to someone else. In line 12 Yuki answers about herself: nanka mou, genki ga demashita (I feel better already), and in line 15 Satou-san says her cheerfulness gives him a lift too (watashi mo genki ga demasu yo). For 'that cheered me up', use the past genki deta. Don't answer genki dashite, which would tell Mai to cheer up, and genki ni natta usually means you've got over an illness. Mai is a friend, so answer casually.
5. **A**. In line 10 Yuki sees the persimmons and says uwaa, tenshon agarimasu! (wow, I'm getting all excited!). Satou-san repeats it in line 11 as a fun, young-sounding expression and tries it himself in lines 16-17. B is the English speaker's trap: koufun shimasu sounds agitated or even sexual, not happily excited. C is the opposite, tenshon sagaru (get bummed out), which doesn't fit someone who loves persimmons. And tenshon is mood or hype, not 'tension' (that's kinchou).
6. Tell him stress has built up, politely since he's your manager: sutoresu ga tamatteru n desu. Stress piles up (tamaru); it isn't a verb you do. For example: *jitsu wa, saikin chotto sutoresu ga tamatteru n desu.* (To be honest, the stress has been piling up a bit lately.); *daijoubu desu. demo, chotto sutoresu ga tamatte kimashita.* (I'm OK. But the stress is starting to build up a bit.). In line 4 Yuki tells Satou-san that after a busy week, sutoresu ga tamatteru n desu (the stress has piled up); he picks it up in line 5 (sutoresu ga tamaru to, when stress piles up) and again in line 13. Use tamatteru for how you are now. Don't say sutoresu shiteru: stress isn't something you do in Japanese, it's something that piles up. Hear the contrast in the scene: stress piles up (tamaru), while Yuki's mood goes up (tenshon agaru, line 10). Tanaka-san is your manager, so answer politely.

| # | Speaker | Romaji | English |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Satou-san | ohayou gozaimasu, yuki-san. kyou wa sentakumono ga yoku kawakisou desu ne. | Good morning, Yuki. Looks like a good day for drying laundry. |
| 2 | Yuki | ohayou gozaimasu. sou desu ne. demo kyou wa nanka, yaruki ga nai n desu yo ne. | Good morning. It does. But today I just can't be bothered. |
| 3 | Satou-san | dou shita n desu ka? shigoto ga isogashikatta n desu ka? | What's the matter? Has work been busy? |
| 4 | Yuki | hai. konshuu wa zutto isogashikute, sutoresu ga tamatteru n desu. | Yes. It's been non-stop all week, and the stress has really piled up. |
| 5 | Satou-san | sore wa taihen deshita ne. sutoresu ga tamaru to, yaruki mo naku narimasu yo ne. | That must have been hard. When stress piles up, your motivation goes too, doesn't it? |
| 6 | Yuki | kyou wa kaji o shiyou to omotteta n desu kedo, zenzen yaruki ga denakute. | I meant to do the housework today, but I just can't get myself going. |
| 7 | Satou-san | yaruki wa ne, hajimetara dete kuru mono desu yo. mazu wa chotto dake, yatte mitara dou desu ka? | Motivation, you know, comes once you've started. Why not try doing just a little first? |
| 8 | Yuki | hajimetara, desu ka. demo, sono hajimeru yaruki ga nai n desu yo. | Once I've started, huh? But I don't even have the motivation to start. |
| 9 | Satou-san | jaa, uchi no kaki, douzo. tabete, genki dashite kudasai. | Well then, have some persimmons from my tree. Eat them and cheer up. |
| 10 | Yuki | e, kaki! daisuki na n desu. uwaa, tenshon agarimasu! | Oh, persimmons! I love them. Wow, I'm getting all excited! |
| 11 | Satou-san | tenshon ga agaru, desu ka. omoshiroi iikata desu ne. | "Tenshon ga agaru," is it? What a fun way to put it. |
| 12 | Yuki | arigatou gozaimasu. nanka mou, genki ga demashita. | Thank you. I feel better already. |
| 13 | Satou-san | sore wa yokatta. sutoresu ga tamatta toki wa, oishii mono ga ichiban desu yo. | I'm glad. When stress builds up, something tasty is the best cure. |
| 14 | Yuki | a, yaruki mo chotto dete kimashita. mazu sentakumono, hoshite kimasu! | Oh, I'm even getting a bit motivated. I'll go and hang out my laundry first! |
| 15 | Satou-san | ii desu ne. yuki-san ga genki da to, watashi mo genki ga demasu yo. | Good for you. When you're cheerful, it gives me a lift too. |
| 16 | Yuki | e, jaa satou-san mo, tenshon agarimashita? | Oh, so you're all hyped up too, Satou-san? |
| 17 | Satou-san | ee, tenshon agarimashita yo. hajimete tsukaimashita. | Yes, very hyped. That's the first time I've ever said it. |

</details>

## 78. Yesterday's bento

*Fridge and microwave · Casual dialogue · 17 lines · Mai, Sora*

Sunday lunchtime at Kenta and Sora's flat. Last night Kenta made hot pot for Yuki and Mai. Mai drops by to pick up the glasses she left behind, just as Sora, barely awake, is about to heat up a bento he bought yesterday.

**Collocations:** *reizouko ni ireru* (put in the fridge); *obentou o atatameru* (heat up a boxed lunch); *futa o suru* (put the lid on); *kigen ga kireru* (expire, be past its date).

**Review:** *juuden ga kireru* (battery runs out, dies); *megane o hazusu* (take off glasses).

### Audio script

| Speaker | Voice |
|---|---|
| Mai | female, late 20s, warm, a little shy |
| Sora | male, mid 20s, light voice, playful |

| # | Speaker | Japanese | Kana (if the voice misreads a kanji) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Mai | ソラ、昨日何度も連絡したんだよ。 | そら、きのうなんどもれんらくしたんだよ。 |
| 2 | Sora | ごめん、充電切れてた。どうしたの？ | ごめん、じゅうでんきれてた。どうしたの？ |

| 4 | Sora | ああ、あれマイのか。ほら、これ。 | ああ、あれまいのか。ほら、これ。 |

| 6 | Sora | うん。昨日買ったお弁当、レンジで温めるところ。 | うん。きのうかったおべんとう、れんじであたためるところ。 |
