| 8 | Sora | ううん、ずっとここにあった。鍋食べて、忘れてた。 | ううん、ずっとここにあった。なべたべて、わすれてた。 |
| 9 | Mai | あ、これ、期限切れてるよ。昨日の夜十時まで。 | あ、これ、きげんきれてるよ。きのうのよるじゅうじまで。 |

| 11 | Mai | だめだよ。冷蔵庫に入れてなかったし、期限も切れてるんだから。 | だめだよ。れいぞうこにいれてなかったし、きげんもきれてるんだから。 |
| 12 | Sora | ああ、お弁当温めて食べるつもりだったのに。 | ああ、おべんとうあたためてたべるつもりだったのに。 |
| 13 | Mai | 昨日の鍋、まだあるんじゃない？ | きのうのなべ、まだあるんじゃない？ |
| 14 | Sora | あ、そうだ。ケンタが蓋して、冷蔵庫に入れといてくれたんだ。 | あ、そうだ。けんたがふたして、れいぞうこにいれといてくれたんだ。 |
| 15 | Mai | ほんとだ、まだいっぱいある。さすがケンタ。 | ほんとだ、まだいっぱいある。さすがけんた。 |
| 16 | Sora | じゃあ、蓋して、五分ぐらい温めよう。 | じゃあ、ふたして、ごふんぐらいあたためよう。 |
| 17 | Mai | うん。今度から、お弁当はすぐ冷蔵庫に入れといてね。 | うん。こんどから、おべんとうはすぐれいぞうこにいれといてね。 |

### Questions

1. **What happened with Sora's lunch?**  
   sora no ohiru wa, dou narimashita ka? (ソラのお昼は、どうなりましたか？)
   - A. The microwave wasn't working, so they heated his bento up in a pot instead.
   - B. His bento had been left out all night and was past its date, so they heated up the leftover hot pot instead.
   - C. Kenta had already finished the leftover hot pot, so Sora heated up his bento after all.

2. **Sora came over for hot pot at your place, and there's a lot left. He asks, "nabe, mada ippai aru ne. dou suru?" Say you'll put it in the fridge, using reizouko ni ireru.** *(say it)*  
   nabe, mada ippai aru ne. dou suru? (鍋、まだいっぱいあるね。どうする？)

3. **Mai tells Sora his bento is past its date. What does Sora say about it?**  
   sora wa, obentou ni tsuite nan to iimashita ka? (ソラは、お弁当について何と言いましたか？)
   - A. It would be fine if he put it in the fridge for a while first.
   - B. It tastes fine cold, so there's no need to heat it.
   - C. It would be fine to eat as long as he heated it up.

4. **There's still plenty of hot pot left. What does Sora say as they heat it up?**  
   nabe o atatameru toki, sora wa nan to iimashita ka? (鍋を温めるとき、ソラは何と言いましたか？)
   - A. *jaa, futa shite, gofun gurai atatameyou.* (lid on, and heat it for about five minutes)
   - B. *jaa, futa oite, gofun gurai atatameyou.* (set the lid down, and heat it for about five minutes)
   - C. *jaa, futa totte, gofun gurai atatameyou.* (lid off, and heat it for about five minutes)

5. **Sora offers you a bento from yesterday. You check the label: its date ran out last night. He asks, "kore, kinou no obentou da kedo, taberu?" Tell him it's past its date, using kigen ga kireru.** *(say it)*  
   kore, kinou no obentou da kedo, taberu? (これ、昨日のお弁当だけど、食べる？)

### New words

- **nabe** (鍋): hot pot; a cooking pot
- **renji** (レンジ): microwave

<details>
<summary>Answers and transcript</summary>

1. **B**. Sora bought the bento yesterday and it sat out all night (lines 6-8). Mai finds its date ran out at ten last night (line 9) and won't let him eat it, even heated (lines 10-11). So they heat up the hot pot Kenta put in the fridge (lines 13-16). A is wrong: the microwave is never a problem, the bento itself is. C is wrong: the hot pot is still there, and plenty of it (line 15).
2. Say it goes in the fridge: reizouko ni ireru, with ni for where the food goes. With -toku it means putting it in there for later: reizouko ni iretoku ne (I'll put it in the fridge). For example: *jaa, reizouko ni iretoku ne.* (I'll put it in the fridge, then.); *futa shite, reizouko ni irete okou. ashita mo taberareru shi.* (Let's put the lid on and put it in the fridge. We can have it tomorrow too.). Mai asks 'reizouko ni ireta?' (did you put it in the fridge?) in line 7, Sora says Kenta 'reizouko ni iretoite kureta' (put it in the fridge for us) in line 14, and Mai ends with 'reizouko ni iretoite ne' in line 17. The verb is ireru, put inside; oku, the English 'put', is setting something down on a surface. Keep ni: it marks where the food goes, unlike satou o ireru, where o marks what goes in.
3. **C**. Line 10: 'obentou wa atatamereba daijoubu da yo' (a bento's fine if you heat it up). atatameru is heating food up, and -ba makes it 'if you heat it'. A is wrong: he says nothing about the fridge, and the bento had never been in it, which is Mai's point in line 11. B is wrong: he wants it hot. In line 6 he was about to heat it in the microwave ('renji de atatameru tokoro'), and in line 12 he says he'd planned to heat it up and eat it.
4. **A**. Line 16: 'futa shite' (put the lid on). A lid goes on with suru, the same suru as masuku o suru, and Kenta did the same before the pot went in the fridge (line 14: 'futa shite, reizouko ni iretoite kureta'). B is the English 'put the lid on' turned word for word into futa o oku, which means setting the lid down somewhere, so the pot would heat uncovered. C, futa o toru, takes the lid off: the opposite of what Sora does after Mai has looked inside.
5. Say it's past its date: kigen kireteru (ga often drops in casual talk), with kireru, the verb for something running out. Then turn it down. For example: *uun, sore kigen kireteru yo.* (No, that's past its date.); *a, kigen ga kireteru kara, yametoku.* (Oh, it's past its date, so I'll pass.). Mai says 'kigen kireteru yo' in line 9 and 'kigen mo kireteru' in line 11, and Sora's 'kigen chotto kireta dake desho?' in line 10 is the past form. 'Expire' needs no special verb: a date runs out with kireru, just as Sora's phone battery did in line 2 ('juuden kireteta'). Don't mix it up with kigen ga warui, 'in a bad mood', which is a different kigen.

| # | Speaker | Romaji | English |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Mai | sora, kinou nando mo renraku shita n da yo. | Sora, I messaged you loads of times last night. |
| 2 | Sora | gomen, juuden kireteta. dou shita no? | Sorry, my battery was dead. What's up? |
| 3 | Mai | megane, koko ni nai? kinou, nabe no toki hazushite, wasurechatta. | Are my glasses here? I took them off while we were having hot pot and left them behind. |
| 4 | Sora | aa, are mai no ka. hora, kore. | Oh, so those were yours. Here you go. |
| 5 | Mai | yokatta, arigatou. are, ima kara ohiru? | Phew, thanks. Oh, are you just having lunch? |
| 6 | Sora | un. kinou katta obentou, renji de atatameru tokoro. | Yeah. I'm about to microwave the bento I bought yesterday. |
| 7 | Mai | kinou? sore, reizouko ni ireta? | Yesterday? Did you put it in the fridge? |
| 8 | Sora | uun, zutto koko ni atta. nabe tabete, wasureteta. | Nope, it's been sitting here the whole time. We had hot pot and I forgot all about it. |
| 9 | Mai | a, kore, kigen kireteru yo. kinou no yoru juuji made. | Oh, this is past its date. It was only good till ten last night. |
| 10 | Sora | demo, kigen chotto kireta dake desho? obentou wa atatamereba daijoubu da yo. | But it's only just past, right? A bento's fine if you heat it up. |
| 11 | Mai | dame da yo. reizouko ni iretenakatta shi, kigen mo kireteru n da kara. | No way. It wasn't in the fridge, and it's past its date as well. |
| 12 | Sora | aa, obentou atatamete taberu tsumori datta noni. | Aw, and I was going to heat it up and have it for lunch. |
| 13 | Mai | kinou no nabe, mada aru n ja nai? | Isn't there still some hot pot left from last night? |
| 14 | Sora | a, sou da. kenta ga futa shite, reizouko ni iretoite kureta n da. | Oh, right. Kenta put the lid on and stuck it in the fridge for us. |
| 15 | Mai | honto da, mada ippai aru. sasuga kenta. | So there is, and loads of it. Good old Kenta. |
| 16 | Sora | jaa, futa shite, gofun gurai atatameyou. | Right, lid on, and we'll heat it for about five minutes. |
| 17 | Mai | un. kondo kara, obentou wa sugu reizouko ni iretoite ne. | OK. And from now on, put your bento straight in the fridge, alright? |

## 79. Glasses Off

*Pride and teasing · Casual dialogue · 18 lines · Okaasan, Kenji*

Late afternoon at home. Kenji, a high-school boy, comes in from school without his glasses, and his mother notices straight away.

**Collocations:** *baka ni suru* (make fun of, look down on); *haji o kaku* (embarrass yourself, make a fool of yourself); *kakko tsukeru* (show off, act cool).

**Review:** *megane o kakeru* (wear / put on glasses); *megane o hazusu* (take off glasses); *koe o kakeru* (speak to, call out to; ask along).

### Audio script

| Speaker | Voice |
|---|---|
| Okaasan | female, 50s, warm, motherly |
| Kenji | male, late teens, sleepy, mumbling |

| # | Speaker | Japanese | Kana (if the voice misreads a kanji) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Okaasan | あれ？ケンジ、メガネかけてないじゃない。どうしたの？ | あれ？けんじ、めがねかけてないじゃない。どうしたの？ |
| 2 | Kenji | 今日、学校でずっとメガネ外してた。 | きょう、がっこうでずっとめがねはずしてた。 |
| 3 | Okaasan | え、なんで？メガネないと、何も見えないでしょ？ | え、なんで？めがねないと、なにもみえないでしょ？ |
| 4 | Kenji | 別に。メガネないほうが、いいかなって。 | べつに。めがねないほうが、いいかなって。 |
| 5 | Okaasan | あ、わかった。かっこつけたかったんでしょ。好きな子でもいるの？ | あ、わかった。かっこつけたかったんでしょ。すきなこでもいるの？ |
| 6 | Kenji | ちがうって。それより、今日はみんなの前で恥をかいたんだよ。 | ちがうって。それより、きょうはみんなのまえではじをかいたんだよ。 |
| 7 | Okaasan | え、何があったの？ | え、なにがあったの？ |
| 8 | Kenji | 朝、あの子だと思って、かっこつけて声かけたんだ。 | あさ、あのこだとおもって、かっこつけてこえかけたんだ。 |
| 9 | Okaasan | あの子？やっぱりいるんじゃない。それで？ | あのこ？やっぱりいるんじゃない。それで？ |
| 10 | Kenji | それが、先生だった。友だちにめっちゃバカにされた。 | それが、せんせいだった。ともだちにめっちゃばかにされた。 |
| 11 | Okaasan | え、先生に？それはおもしろいね。 | え、せんせいに？それはおもしろいね。 |
| 12 | Kenji | お母さんまでバカにしないでよ。 | おかあさんまでばかにしないでよ。 |
| 13 | Okaasan | ごめんごめん、バカにしてないよ。恥ずかしかったね。 | ごめんごめん、ばかにしてないよ。はずかしかったね。 |
| 14 | Kenji | うん。あんなに恥をかいたの、初めてだよ。 | うん。あんなにはじをかいたの、はじめてだよ。 |
| 15 | Okaasan | だから、かっこつけなくていいのよ。メガネかけてても、ケンジはかっこいいんだから。 | だから、かっこつけなくていいのよ。めがねかけてても、けんじはかっこいいんだから。 |
| 16 | Kenji | それ、あとであの子にも言われた。メガネかけてるほうが、かっこいいって。 | それ、あとであのこにもいわれた。めがねかけてるほうが、かっこいいって。 |
| 17 | Okaasan | ほらね。じゃあ、明日はちゃんとメガネかけて行きなさい。 | ほらね。じゃあ、あしたはちゃんとめがねかけていきなさい。 |
| 18 | Kenji | うん。もう恥はかきたくないし。 | うん。もうはじはかきたくないし。 |

### Questions

1. **What happened?**  
   nani ga arimashita ka? (何がありましたか？)
   - A. Kenji's friends made fun of his glasses, so he's going to stop wearing them to school.
   - B. Kenji took his glasses off to look cool, mistook a teacher for the girl he likes, and will wear them again tomorrow.
   - C. Kenji played it cool with the girl he likes, but she told him he looks better without glasses.

2. **You tell Kenji you don't know how to send a photo from your new phone. He smirks and says, "e, sore mo shiranai no? uso desho?" Tell him not to make fun of you, using baka ni suru.** *(say it)*  
   e, sore mo shiranai no? uso desho? (え、それも知らないの？うそでしょ？)

3. **At a meeting today you called a new coworker by the wrong name in front of everyone. When you get home, your mother asks, "dou shita no? genki nai ne." Tell her what happened, using haji o kaku.** *(say it)*  
   dou shita no? genki nai ne. (どうしたの？元気ないね。)

4. **When Kenji says he kept his glasses off all day, his mother says, "kakko tsuketakatta n desho." What does she mean?**  
   okaasan wa, kenji no koto o dou omoimashita ka? (お母さんは、ケンジのことをどう思いましたか？)
   - A. She thinks he was bragging to his friends about something.
   - B. She's telling him he looks cool without his glasses.
   - C. She thinks he was trying to look cool, maybe for a girl.

### New words

- **mieru** (見える): can see, be visible (mienai: can't see)
- **ko** (子): kid; here, a girl (suki na ko: a girl you like; ano ko: that girl, her)
- **sensei** (先生): teacher
- **kakkoii** (かっこいい): cool, good-looking (being cool, as opposed to kakko tsukeru, acting cool)

<details>
<summary>Answers and transcript</summary>

1. **B**. Lines 1-5: Kenji kept his glasses off all day at school, and his mother guesses he wanted to look cool for a girl. Lines 8-10: he called out to someone he took for the girl, but it was a teacher, and his friends made fun of him. Lines 16-18: the girl later told him he looks cooler with his glasses on, so he'll wear them tomorrow. A is wrong: his friends made fun of him for greeting a teacher, not for his glasses, and he's going back to wearing them (lines 17-18). C is wrong: the person he greeted was a teacher, not the girl (line 10), and the girl said he looks cooler with glasses, not without (line 16).
2. Protest with baka ni shinaide (yo), 'don't make fun of me', the way Kenji says it to his mother. Or call him out with baka ni shiteru desho (you're making fun of me, aren't you). For example: *chotto, baka ni shinaide yo. shiranai mono wa shiranai n da kara.* (Hey, don't make fun of me. I just don't know, all right?); *baka ni shiteru desho. jaa, kenji ga oshiete yo.* (You're making fun of me, aren't you? Fine, then you show me.). After his friends made fun of him (tomodachi ni metcha baka ni sareta, line 10), Kenji tells his mother okaasan made baka ni shinaide yo when she finds the story funny (line 12), and she answers baka ni shitenai yo (line 13). baka ni suru is mocking someone or treating them as stupid, which is what Kenji's smirk does here. karakau (tease) is lighter, for playful teasing, and warau only means laugh, so warawanaide is 'don't laugh', not 'don't make fun of me'. Keep the ni: baka ni suru, literally 'treat as a fool'.
3. Say you made a fool of yourself in front of everyone: minna no mae de haji o kaita (or haji o kaichatta). To say how it felt, add hazukashikatta. For example: *un. kyou, kaigi de haji o kaichatta. metcha hazukashikatta.* (Yeah. I made a fool of myself in a meeting today. It was so embarrassing.); *minna no mae de haji kaita no. atarashii hito no namae, machigaechatta.* (I embarrassed myself in front of everyone. I got the new person's name wrong.). Kenji uses haji o kaku three times about his mix-up: minna no mae de haji o kaita (line 6), anna ni haji o kaita no, hajimete (line 14) and mou haji wa kakitakunai (line 18). haji o kaku is the incident; how it felt is hazukashii, which his mother says in line 13 (hazukashikatta ne). 'I embarrassed myself' word for word, jibun o hazukashiku shita, isn't said in Japanese. This kaku is the one in ase o kaku (break out in sweat), not kaku 'write'. In casual speech the o can drop (haji kaita), and -chatta adds that it went wrong.
4. **C**. Line 5: kakko tsukeru is putting on a cool act, trying to look good, and she follows it with suki na ko demo iru no? (is there a girl you like?). Kenji does it again in line 8 (kakko tsukete koe kaketa: played it cool and said hi), and in line 15 she tells him kakko tsukenakute ii (you don't need to act cool). A is jiman suru, boasting in words about what you have or did, and nobody brags here. B mixes it up with kakkoii, actually being cool: in line 15 she says he's cool with his glasses on (megane kaketete mo, kenji wa kakkoii), not without them.

| # | Speaker | Romaji | English |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Okaasan | are? kenji, megane kaketenai ja nai. dou shita no? | Huh? Kenji, you're not wearing your glasses. What happened? |
| 2 | Kenji | kyou, gakkou de zutto megane hazushiteta. | I had them off all day at school today. |
| 3 | Okaasan | e, nande? megane nai to, nanimo mienai desho? | What, why? You can't see a thing without them, can you? |
| 4 | Kenji | betsu ni. megane nai hou ga, ii kana tte. | No reason. I just thought I might look better without them. |
| 5 | Okaasan | a, wakatta. kakko tsuketakatta n desho. suki na ko demo iru no? | Oh, I get it. You wanted to look cool, didn't you? Is there a girl you like or something? |
| 6 | Kenji | chigau tte. sore yori, kyou wa minna no mae de haji o kaita n da yo. | I said no. Anyway, I made a fool of myself in front of everyone today. |
| 7 | Okaasan | e, nani ga atta no? | Oh no, what happened? |
| 8 | Kenji | asa, ano ko da to omotte, kakko tsukete koe kaketa n da. | This morning I thought I saw her, so I played it cool and said hi. |
| 9 | Okaasan | ano ko? yappari iru n ja nai. sore de? | Her? So there is someone! And then? |
| 10 | Kenji | sore ga, sensei datta. tomodachi ni metcha baka ni sareta. | It was a teacher. My friends totally made fun of me. |
| 11 | Okaasan | e, sensei ni? sore wa omoshiroi ne. | What, to a teacher? Well, that's funny. |
| 12 | Kenji | okaasan made baka ni shinaide yo. | Don't you make fun of me too, Mom. |
| 13 | Okaasan | gomen gomen, baka ni shitenai yo. hazukashikatta ne. | Sorry, sorry, I'm not making fun of you. That must have been embarrassing. |
| 14 | Kenji | un. anna ni haji o kaita no, hajimete da yo. | Yeah. I've never made such a fool of myself. |
| 15 | Okaasan | dakara, kakko tsukenakute ii no yo. megane kaketete mo, kenji wa kakkoii n da kara. | That's why you don't need to act cool. You're cool with your glasses on too, you know. |
| 16 | Kenji | sore, ato de ano ko ni mo iwareta. megane kaketeru hou ga, kakkoii tte. | She said that too, later. That I look cooler with my glasses on. |
| 17 | Okaasan | hora ne. jaa, ashita wa chanto megane kakete ikinasai. | See? Then tomorrow, you wear your glasses to school. |
| 18 | Kenji | un. mou haji wa kakitakunai shi. | Yeah. I don't want to make a fool of myself again anyway. |

</details>

## 80. Rained Off

*Exercise and outdoors · Polite monologue · 12 lines · Satou-san*

A Sunday evening. Satou-san, the elderly neighbour, leaves a voicemail for Kenta, who lent him a tent for a camping trip with some old friends. Rain spoiled part of their plan, and Satou-san has an idea for next Saturday.

**Collocations:** *karada o ugokasu* (get some exercise, move your body); *kibun tenkan ni naru* (be a nice change of pace, do someone good); *yama ni noboru* (climb a mountain); *tsuri ni iku* (go fishing); *kyanpu ni iku* (go camping).

**Review:** *toshi o toru* (get older, age); *sutoresu ga tamaru* (get stressed, stress builds up).

### Audio script

| Speaker | Voice |
|---|---|
| Satou-san | male, 70s, polite, kind, unhurried |

| # | Speaker | Japanese | Kana (if the voice misreads a kanji) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Satou-san | もしもし、ケンタくん？佐藤です。テント、貸してくれてありがとうございました。 | もしもし、けんたくん？さとうです。てんと、かしてくれてありがとうございました。 |
| 2 | Satou-san | 昨日から、昔の友達とキャンプに行ってたんです。さっき帰ってきました。 | きのうから、むかしのともだちときゃんぷにいってたんです。さっきかえってきました。 |
| 3 | Satou-san | 本当は、昨日みんなで山に登るつもりだったんですけど、雨でだめでした。 | ほんとうは、きのうみんなでやまにのぼるつもりだったんですけど、あめでだめでした。 |
| 4 | Satou-san | それで、代わりに釣りに行ったんです。まあ、何も釣れませんでしたけどね。 | それで、かわりにつりにいったんです。まあ、なにもつれませんでしたけどね。 |
| 5 | Satou-san | でも、みんなでずっと話してて、いい気分転換になりましたよ。 | でも、みんなでずっとはなしてて、いいきぶんてんかんになりましたよ。 |
| 6 | Satou-san | そういえばケンタくん、もう三年もキャンプに行ってないって、言ってましたよね。 | そういえばけんたくん、もうさんねんもきゃんぷにいってないって、いってましたよね。 |
| 7 | Satou-san | 最近は仕事ばっかりで、全然体を動かしてないって。 | さいきんはしごとばっかりで、ぜんぜんからだをうごかしてないって。 |
| 8 | Satou-san | 来週の土曜日、みんなでもう一度、あの山に登るんです。ケンタくんも一緒にどうですか？ | らいしゅうのどようび、みんなでもういちど、あのやまにのぼるんです。けんたくんもいっしょにどうですか？ |
| 9 | Satou-san | 小さい山ですから、年をとった私でも登れます。でも、ちゃんと体を動かせますよ。 | ちいさいやまですから、としをとったわたしでものぼれます。でも、ちゃんとからだをうごかせますよ。 |
| 10 | Satou-san | 仕事でストレスもたまってるでしょう？たまには気分転換しないと。 | しごとですとれすもたまってるでしょう？たまにはきぶんてんかんしないと。 |
| 11 | Satou-san | 山に登ってから、また釣りに行くつもりです。それもいい気分転換になりますよ。 | やまにのぼってから、またつりにいくつもりです。それもいいきぶんてんかんになりますよ。 |
| 12 | Satou-san | あ、土曜日は朝四時に出ますからね。じゃあ、連絡待ってます。 | あ、どようびはあさよじにでますからね。じゃあ、れんらくまってます。 |

### Questions

1. **Why does Satou-san call Kenta?**  
   satou-san wa, doushite kenta-kun ni denwa shimashita ka? (佐藤さんは、どうしてケンタくんに電話しましたか？)
   - A. To say the rain spoiled the whole trip, so he's bringing the tent back and staying home next weekend.
   - B. To thank him for the tent and invite him to climb the mountain with them next Saturday, after rain stopped them this time.
   - C. To tell him they caught lots of fish, and ask him to come fishing with them early tomorrow morning.

2. **Your friend Sora has spent all Sunday indoors. In the evening he stretches and groans, "kyou mo zutto uchi de geemu shiteta. nanka karada ga darui naa." Tell him he needs to get some exercise now and then, using karada o ugokasu.** *(say it)*  
   kyou mo zutto uchi de geemu shiteta. nanka karada ga darui naa. (今日もずっとうちでゲームしてた。なんか体がだるいなあ。)

3. **Your manager Tanaka-san looks worn out on Friday afternoon and says, "saikin isogashikute, shuumatsu mo shigoto no koto bakari kangaete shimau n desu yo." Suggest something for the weekend and tell him it'll do him good, using kibun tenkan ni naru.** *(say it)*  
   saikin isogashikute, shuumatsu mo shigoto no koto bakari kangaete shimau n desu yo. (最近忙しくて、週末も仕事のことばかり考えてしまうんですよ。)

4. **On Monday your friend Yuki says, "shuumatsu, nani shiteta no? zutto renraku nakatta kedo." You didn't answer because you were climbing a mountain with friends on Saturday. Tell her, using yama ni noboru.** *(say it)*  
   shuumatsu, nani shiteta no? zutto renraku nakatta kedo. (週末、何してたの？ずっと連絡なかったけど。)

5. **When rain stopped the climb, what did Satou-san and his friends do instead?**  
   satou-san-tachi wa, kawari ni nani o shimashita ka? (佐藤さんたちは、代わりに何をしましたか？)
   - A. They climbed a smaller mountain nearby.
   - B. They went fishing and caught enough for dinner.
   - C. They went fishing, but nobody caught anything.

6. **What did Satou-san say Kenta hadn't done for three years?**  
   satou-san wa, kenta-kun no koto o nan to iimashita ka? (佐藤さんは、ケンタくんのことを何と言いましたか？)
   - A. *mou sannen mo kyanpu ni ittenai* (He hasn't been camping in three whole years.)
   - B. *mou sannen mo kyanpingu ni ittenai* (He hasn't gone 'camping' in three whole years (the English -ing word).)
   - C. *mou sannen mo tsuri ni ittenai* (He hasn't been fishing in three whole years.)

### New words

- **tento** (テント): tent
- **tsureru** (釣れる): (of a fish) be caught, bite: nanimo tsuremasen deshita = we didn't catch a thing
- **-kun** (くん): name suffix for a younger man or boy (kenta-kun)

<details>
<summary>Answers and transcript</summary>

1. **B**. Line 1: Satou-san thanks Kenta for lending him the tent. Lines 3-4: rain stopped them climbing the mountain, so they went fishing instead. Line 8: they're all having another go at that mountain next Saturday, and he asks Kenta to come, since Kenta said he hadn't been getting any exercise (line 7). A is wrong: the rain only stopped the climb, the trip was still a nice change of pace (line 5), and he's going out again next Saturday and wants Kenta along. C is wrong: they didn't catch a thing (line 4), and the outing is next Saturday, leaving at four in the morning (line 12), not tomorrow.
2. Tell him to get moving: tama ni wa karada (o) ugokasanakya (you've got to get some exercise now and then), or suggest doing it together with karada o ugokasou. It's ugokasu, with o. For example: *zutto uchi ni iru kara da yo. tama ni wa karada ugokasanakya.* (That's because you've been stuck indoors all day. You've got to get some exercise now and then.); *ashita, issho ni soto de karada o ugokasou yo. sukoshi demo zenzen chigau kara.* (Let's go out and get some exercise together tomorrow. Even a little makes a big difference.). Satou-san reminds Kenta he said he hadn't been moving his body at all (zenzen karada o ugokashitenai, line 7), and promises the small mountain still gives a proper workout (chanto karada o ugokasemasu, line 9). karada o ugokasu is the everyday way to talk about getting exercise, anything from a walk to a climb, and casual speech often drops the o (karada ugokasanakya). ugokasu means move something, so it takes o; ugoku is move by itself, so karada o ugoku is wrong, and karada ga ugokanai means you physically can't move. undou suru (exercise) works too, but it leans towards sport and the gym.
3. Suggest an outing (tsuri ni itte mitara dou desu ka?) and add ii kibun tenkan ni narimasu yo (it'd be a nice change of pace). Keep it polite with Tanaka-san: narimasu, not naru. For example: *jaa, shuumatsu wa tsuri ni itte mitara dou desu ka? ii kibun tenkan ni narimasu yo.* (Then why not try going fishing this weekend? It'd be a nice change of pace.); *tama ni wa yama ni nobotte mitara dou desu ka? kitto ii kibun tenkan ni narimasu yo.* (How about climbing a mountain for once? I'm sure it'd do you good.). Satou-san says the rained-off trip still made ii kibun tenkan ni narimashita (a nice change of pace, line 5), and that fishing after the climb will be ii kibun tenkan ni narimasu too (line 11). kibun tenkan ni naru, usually with ii, says something does you good as a break, which makes it the natural phrase for encouraging someone. kibun tenkan suru is taking the break yourself: in line 10 Satou-san tells Kenta tama ni wa kibun tenkan shinai to (you need to take a break now and then). kibarashi ni naru works the same way, but leans towards taking your mind off something bad.
4. Say you were up a mountain with friends: tomodachi to yama ni nobotteta (n da), or yama ni nobotta. Keep the ni. For example: *gomen gomen, tomodachi to yama ni nobotteta n da.* (Sorry, sorry! I was off climbing a mountain with some friends.); *doyoubi ni, tomodachi to yama ni nobotta n da. ue made sanjikan mo kakatta yo.* (I climbed a mountain with friends on Saturday. It took three whole hours to reach the top.). Satou-san had planned yama ni noboru tsumori (line 3), says they'll climb ano yama ni noboru next Saturday (line 8), and plans to go fishing again yama ni nobotte kara (after the climb, line 11). English 'climb a mountain' pulls you towards yama o noboru, which is also heard when the way up is the point (a steep path), but for having been up a mountain ni is about three times as common. noboremasu in line 9 is the can-do form: toshi o totta watashi demo noboremasu, even an old man like me can climb it. nobotteta is 'was climbing', a natural excuse for not answering.
5. **C**. Line 4: sorede, kawari ni tsuri ni itta n desu (so we went fishing instead), then nanimo tsuremasen deshita (we didn't catch a thing). tsuri ni iku puts the activity straight before ni iku, and tsureru is a fish getting caught on the line, so the negative means nothing bit. B misses that negative. A: nobody climbed anything; rain called off the climb (line 3), and they'll try the same mountain next Saturday (line 8), with fishing again afterwards (mata tsuri ni iku tsumori, line 11).
6. **A**. Line 6: sou ieba kenta-kun, mou sannen mo kyanpu ni ittenai tte, ittemashita yo ne. ittenai is itte (i)nai, haven't gone, and Satou-san says the same of his own trip in line 2: kyanpu ni itteta n desu (I was away camping). B copies the English '-ing': the activity is kyanpu, and kyanpingu turns up mostly in compounds like kyanpingu kaa (camper van). C: fishing is what Satou-san did on his trip and plans for next Saturday; what Kenta hadn't done in three years was camp, which is why his tent was free to lend.

| # | Speaker | Romaji | English |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Satou-san | moshimoshi, kenta-kun? satou desu. tento, kashite kurete arigatou gozaimashita. | Hello, Kenta? It's Satou. Thank you for lending me your tent. |
| 2 | Satou-san | kinou kara, mukashi no tomodachi to kyanpu ni itteta n desu. sakki kaette kimashita. | I've been away camping with some old friends since yesterday. I just got back. |
| 3 | Satou-san | hontou wa, kinou minna de yama ni noboru tsumori datta n desu kedo, ame de dame deshita. | We'd actually planned to climb a mountain together yesterday, but the rain put a stop to that. |
| 4 | Satou-san | sorede, kawari ni tsuri ni itta n desu. maa, nanimo tsuremasen deshita kedo ne. | So we went fishing instead. Not that we caught a thing. |
| 5 | Satou-san | demo, minna de zutto hanashitete, ii kibun tenkan ni narimashita yo. | But we chatted the whole time, and it made a really nice change of pace. |
| 6 | Satou-san | sou ieba kenta-kun, mou sannen mo kyanpu ni ittenai tte, ittemashita yo ne. | Come to think of it, Kenta, you told me you hadn't been camping in three whole years. |
| 7 | Satou-san | saikin wa shigoto bakkari de, zenzen karada o ugokashitenai tte. | And that lately it's been all work, and you haven't been moving your body at all. |
| 8 | Satou-san | raishuu no doyoubi, minna de mou ichido, ano yama ni noboru n desu. kenta-kun mo issho ni dou desu ka? | Next Saturday we're all having another go at that mountain. Why don't you come along? |
| 9 | Satou-san | chiisai yama desu kara, toshi o totta watashi demo noboremasu. demo, chanto karada o ugokasemasu yo. | It's a small one, so even an old man like me can climb it. But you'll still get some proper exercise. |
| 10 | Satou-san | shigoto de sutoresu mo tamatteru deshou? tama ni wa kibun tenkan shinai to. | Work must be getting you stressed, too. You need a change of pace now and then. |
| 11 | Satou-san | yama ni nobotte kara, mata tsuri ni iku tsumori desu. sore mo ii kibun tenkan ni narimasu yo. | After the climb, we're planning to go fishing again. That'll do you good, too. |
| 12 | Satou-san | a, doyoubi wa asa yoji ni demasu kara ne. jaa, renraku mattemasu. | Oh, and on Saturday we leave at four in the morning. Well, let me know. |

</details>

## 81. No signal at the bus stop

*Internet and apps · Casual dialogue · 17 lines · Yuki, Kenta*

A Saturday afternoon in the countryside. Yuki and Kenta have just hiked down a mountain to a small bus stop at the bottom, and they want to know when the next bus back to town comes.

**Collocations:** *netto de shiraberu* (look something up online); *netto ga tsunagaru* (the internet connects, works); *denpa ga warui* (bad signal, poor reception); *apuri o ireru* (install an app, get an app).

**Review:** *juuden ga kireru* (battery runs out, dies); *dengen o kiru* (turn off (a phone or device)); *dengen o ireru* (turn on (a phone or device)).

### Audio script

| Speaker | Voice |
|---|---|
| Yuki | female, late 20s, bright and friendly |
| Kenta | male, late 20s, relaxed and easygoing |

| # | Speaker | Japanese | Kana (if the voice misreads a kanji) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Kenta | はあ、やっとバス停まで来た。次のバス、何時かな？ | はあ、やっとばすていまできた。つぎのばす、なんじかな？ |
| 2 | Yuki | ちょっと待って。ネットで調べてみる。 | ちょっとまって。ねっとでしらべてみる。 |
| 3 | Kenta | ありがとう。スマホ、充電切れちゃってさ。 | ありがとう。すまほ、じゅうでんきれちゃってさ。 |
| 4 | Yuki | あれ？なんかネットつながらないんだけど。 | あれ？なんかねっとつながらないんだけど。 |
| 5 | Kenta | 山の中だからね。ここ、電波悪いんじゃない？ | やまのなかだからね。ここ、でんぱわるいんじゃない？ |
| 6 | Yuki | ほんとだ。電波悪くて、何も見れない。 | ほんとだ。でんぱわるくて、なにもみれない。 |
| 7 | Kenta | じゃあ、バスの時間がわかるアプリとか、入れてないの？ | じゃあ、ばすのじかんがわかるあぷりとか、いれてないの？ |
| 8 | Yuki | 入れてない。それに、アプリもネットつながらないと使えないよ。 | いれてない。それに、あぷりもねっとつながらないとつかえないよ。 |
| 9 | Kenta | そっか。じゃあ、一回電源切って、また入れてみたら？ | そっか。じゃあ、いっかいでんげんきって、またいれてみたら？ |
| 10 | Yuki | だめ。電源入れても、やっぱり電波悪い。 | だめ。でんげんいれても、やっぱりでんぱわるい。 |
| 11 | Kenta | ねえ、ユキ。 | ねえ、ゆき。 |
| 12 | Yuki | 待って。もう一回ネットで調べてみるから。 | まって。もういっかいねっとでしらべてみるから。 |
| 13 | Kenta | いや、ネットで調べなくてもいいかも。ほら、後ろ。 | いや、ねっとでしらべなくてもいいかも。ほら、うしろ。 |
| 14 | Yuki | え？あ、時刻表！ずっとここにあったんだ。 | え？あ、じこくひょう！ずっとここにあったんだ。 |
| 15 | Kenta | 次は3時40分だって。あと10分だよ。 | つぎはさんじよんじゅっぷんだって。あとじゅっぷんだよ。 |
| 16 | Yuki | よかった。でも一応、アプリ入れとく。 | よかった。でもいちおう、あぷりいれとく。 |
| 17 | Kenta | ネットがつながったらね。 | ねっとがつながったらね。 |

### Questions

1. **What happens at the bus stop?**  
   nani ga arimashita ka? (何がありましたか？)
   - A. Yuki's phone works again after she switches it off and on, so she finds the bus time online.
   - B. Kenta's phone has died, so they miss the bus and have to wait another hour.
   - C. They can't get online to check the bus times, then Kenta spots the timetable right there at the stop.

2. **You and Kenta want to see a film tonight. He asks, "nee, kono eiga, nanji kara da kke?" You don't know. Offer to look it up online.** *(say it)*  
   nee, kono eiga, nanji kara da kke? (ねえ、この映画、何時からだっけ？)

3. **When Yuki tries to look up the bus on her phone, something goes wrong. What does she say?**  
   sumaho o mite, yuki-san wa nan to iimashita ka? (スマホを見て、ユキさんは何と言いましたか？)
   - A. *nanka netto tsunagaranai n da kedo* (The internet won't connect for some reason.)
   - B. *nanka netto hatarakanai n da kedo* (The internet isn't working (hataraku: work, as a person does a job).)
   - C. *nanka netto ugokanai n da kedo* (The internet won't move (ugoku, as for a frozen app).)

4. **You're out in the countryside when Kenta calls. The line keeps breaking up, and he says, "moshimoshi? kikoeru? nanka, yoku kikoenai n da kedo." Tell him it's the signal.** *(say it)*  
   moshimoshi? kikoeru? nanka, yoku kikoenai n da kedo. (もしもし？聞こえる？なんか、よく聞こえないんだけど。)

5. **Once they've found the timetable, Yuki says, "demo ichiou, apuri iretoku." What is she going to do?**  
   yuki-san wa, nani o shimasu ka? (ユキさんは、何をしますか？)
   - A. Turn her phone back on and try the internet again.
   - B. Get the bus-times app on her phone anyway, just in case.
   - C. Put her phone away in her bag, since they don't need it now.

### New words

- **basutei** (バス停): bus stop
- **jikokuhyou** (時刻表): timetable (for buses or trains)

<details>
<summary>Answers and transcript</summary>

1. **C**. In line 2 Yuki offers to look up the next bus online (netto de shirabete miru). In line 4 the internet won't connect (netto tsunagaranai), and in lines 5-6 they put it down to the bad signal in the mountains (denpa warui). In lines 9-10 switching the phone off and on doesn't help. In line 13 Kenta says she doesn't need to look it up online and points behind her, and in line 14 Yuki sees that the timetable was there all along. A is wrong: in line 10 the signal is still bad after she turns the phone back on, and they get the time from the timetable, not the internet. B is wrong: Kenta's phone has died (line 3), but they don't miss the bus. The next one comes in ten minutes (line 15).
2. Say you'll look it up online: netto de shirabete miru (let me look it up online), or netto de shiraberu ne. Keep de, because the internet is the tool you look things up with. For example: *wakannai. chotto netto de shirabete miru.* (No idea. Let me look it up online.); *nanji da kke. chotto matte, netto de shiraberu ne.* (What time was it again? Hang on, I'll look it up online.). When Kenta wonders about the bus, Yuki says 'chotto matte. netto de shirabete miru' (line 2) and says it again in line 12. In line 13 Kenta tells her 'netto de shirabenakute mo ii kamo' (no need to look it up online). de marks the internet as the means, the same way as sumaho de or jisho de. The thing you check takes o (eiga no jikan o netto de shiraberu). shiraberu is for checking facts like times and meanings. For finding a place such as a shop, use sagasu (netto de sagasu). Kenta asks casually, so answer casually. Adding -te miru makes it a friendly offer.
3. **A**. In line 4 Yuki says 'are? nanka netto tsunagaranai n da kedo.' When Wi-Fi or mobile data fails, Japanese says the internet doesn't connect (tsunagaranai). Yuki says it again in line 8 (netto tsunagaranai to tsukaenai), and Kenta uses it in line 17 (netto ga tsunagattara ne). B is wrong: hataraku is 'work' in the sense of a person doing a job, so this copies English 'isn't working' word for word. C is wrong: ugokanai (won't move) is for a frozen app or device, not for a connection that won't come up.
4. Apologise and blame the signal with denpa ga warui. In casual speech ga drops: gomen, koko denpa warui n da. To give the bad signal as the reason for something, use the -kute form: denpa warukute. For example: *gomen, koko denpa warui n da. ato de mata kakeru ne.* (Sorry, the signal's bad here. I'll call you back later.); *gomen, denpa warukute, kotchi mo yoku kikoenai.* (Sorry, the signal's bad. I can't hear you well either.). Kenta says 'koko, denpa warui n ja nai?' in line 5. Yuki says 'denpa warukute, nanimo mirenai' in line 6 and 'yappari denpa warui' in line 10. Phone signal is denpa ('radio waves'). Don't say shingou, which means a traffic light, or the loanword shigunaru. warui covers both broken-up calls and pages that won't load. If there's no signal at all, say denpa hairanai. Kenta is a friend, so drop ga and answer casually.
5. **B**. Line 16: apuri iretoku is apuri o ireru (get an app, literally 'put it in') plus -toku (do it ahead of time). It's the kind of app Kenta asked about in line 7 (basu no jikan ga wakaru apuri toka, irete nai no?), and in line 8 she said she didn't have it (irete nai). That's why Kenta jokes in line 17 that she'll have to wait until the internet works. A is wrong: turning the phone on is dengen o ireru, which she already tried in line 10, and here the thing she 'puts in' is the app, not the power. C is wrong: ireru can mean putting something into a bag, but she said apuri, and nobody mentions a bag. Note that English 'get the app' is not apuri o morau, which would sound as if someone handed it to you.

| # | Speaker | Romaji | English |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Kenta | haa, yatto basutei made kita. tsugi no basu, nanji kana? | Phew, we made it to the bus stop at last. What time's the next bus, I wonder? |
| 2 | Yuki | chotto matte. netto de shirabete miru. | Hang on, I'll look it up online. |
| 3 | Kenta | arigatou. sumaho, juuden kirechatte sa. | Thanks. My phone's battery died, see. |
| 4 | Yuki | are? nanka netto tsunagaranai n da kedo. | Huh? The internet's not working for some reason. |
| 5 | Kenta | yama no naka da kara ne. koko, denpa warui n ja nai? | Well, we're out in the mountains. The signal's probably bad here. |
| 6 | Yuki | honto da. denpa warukute, nanimo mirenai. | You're right. The signal's so bad I can't load anything. |
| 7 | Kenta | jaa, basu no jikan ga wakaru apuri toka, irete nai no? | Then haven't you got an app or something that tells you the bus times? |
| 8 | Yuki | irete nai. sore ni, apuri mo netto tsunagaranai to tsukaenai yo. | Nope. Besides, apps don't work either if the internet's down. |
| 9 | Kenta | sokka. jaa, ikkai dengen kitte, mata irete mitara? | Oh, right. Then why not try switching it off and on again? |
| 10 | Yuki | dame. dengen irete mo, yappari denpa warui. | No good. Even after turning it back on, the signal's still bad. |
| 11 | Kenta | nee, yuki. | Hey, Yuki. |
| 12 | Yuki | matte. mou ikkai netto de shirabete miru kara. | Wait. I'm going to try looking it up online one more time. |
| 13 | Kenta | iya, netto de shirabenakute mo ii kamo. hora, ushiro. | No, I don't think you need to look it up online. Look, behind you. |
| 14 | Yuki | e? a, jikokuhyou! zutto koko ni atta n da. | Huh? Oh, the timetable! It was right here the whole time. |
| 15 | Kenta | tsugi wa sanji yonjuppun da tte. ato juppun da yo. | The next one's at 3:40, it says. That's ten minutes from now. |
| 16 | Yuki | yokatta. demo ichiou, apuri iretoku. | Phew. I'll still get the app, though, just in case. |
| 17 | Kenta | netto ga tsunagattara ne. | Once the internet's working, that is. |

</details>

## 82. Camping at home

*Plans and promises · Casual dialogue · 17 lines · Kenta, Sora*

Friday evening in the flat Kenta and Sora share. Kenta has spent a month planning a weekend camping trip for the two of them, and Sora, who dropped out last time when work came up, has sworn he's coming. Then a typhoon turns up in the news.

**Collocations:** *yousu o miru* (wait and see; check on (someone)); *chuushi ni naru* (be called off); *yakusoku o mamoru* (keep a promise); *keikaku o tateru* (make a plan).

**Review:** *shigoto ga hairu* (work comes up); *taifuu ga kuru* (a typhoon comes (hits)); *gohan o tsukuru* (cook a meal, make dinner).

### Audio script

| Speaker | Voice |
|---|---|
| Kenta | male, late 20s, relaxed and easygoing |
| Sora | male, mid 20s, light voice, playful |

| # | Speaker | Japanese | Kana (if the voice misreads a kanji) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Kenta | ソラ、明日のキャンプ、ほんとに大丈夫？今度はちゃんと約束守ってよ。 | そら、あしたのきゃんぷ、ほんとにだいじょうぶ？こんどはちゃんとやくそくまもってよ。 |
| 2 | Sora | 大丈夫だって。前は急に仕事入っちゃっただけだよ。約束は守るよ。 | だいじょうぶだって。まえはきゅうにしごとはいっちゃっただけだよ。やくそくはまもるよ。 |
| 3 | Kenta | ならいいけど。先月から計画立ててたんだからね。 | ならいいけど。せんげつからけいかくたててたんだからね。 |
| 4 | Sora | 知ってるよ。あ、ちょっとこれ見て。明日、台風来るって。 | しってるよ。あ、ちょっとこれみて。あした、たいふうくるって。 |
| 5 | Kenta | え、うそ。でも、まだわかんないよね。とりあえず明日の朝まで様子見よう。 | え、うそ。でも、まだわかんないよね。とりあえずあしたのあさまでようすみよう。 |
| 6 | Sora | うん。あ、見て。日曜日のサッカーの試合、もう中止になったって。 | うん。あ、みて。にちようびのさっかーのしあい、もうちゅうしになったって。 |
| 7 | Kenta | マジで？あ、キャンプ場からメールだ。 | まじで？あ、きゃんぷじょうからめーるだ。 |
| 8 | Sora | え、なんて？ | え、なんて？ |
| 9 | Kenta | だめだ。台風で、キャンプ中止になった。予約もキャンセルしなきゃ。 | だめだ。たいふうで、きゃんぷちゅうしになった。よやくもきゃんせるしなきゃ。 |
| 10 | Sora | 残念。明日まで様子見る必要もなかったね。 | ざんねん。あしたまでようすみるひつようもなかったね。 |
| 11 | Kenta | はあ、せっかく計画立てたのになあ。 | はあ、せっかくけいかくたてたのになあ。 |
| 12 | Sora | じゃあさ、うちでキャンプしようよ。約束は守るって言ったでしょ？ | じゃあさ、うちできゃんぷしようよ。やくそくはまもるっていったでしょ？ |
| 13 | Kenta | うちで？いいけど、計画は？ | うちで？いいけど、けいかくは？ |
| 14 | Sora | 計画は僕が立てる。テントは部屋の中。で、ご飯はケンタが作る。 | けいかくはぼくがたてる。てんとはへやのなか。で、ごはんはけんたがつくる。 |
| 15 | Kenta | ソラが計画立てると、結局僕がご飯作るんだ。 | そらがけいかくたてると、けっきょくぼくがごはんつくるんだ。 |
| 16 | Sora | いいじゃん。ケンタのご飯、おいしいし。 | いいじゃん。けんたのごはん、おいしいし。 |
| 17 | Kenta | わかった、わかった。じゃあ、台風が来ても、うちのキャンプは中止にならないね。 | わかった、わかった。じゃあ、たいふうがきても、うちのきゃんぷはちゅうしにならないね。 |

### Questions

1. **What happens in this conversation?**  
   nani ga arimashita ka? (何がありましたか？)
   - A. Work comes up for Sora again, so Kenta calls off the camping trip and decides to cook at home instead.
   - B. A typhoon gets their camping trip called off, so Sora, who promised to come this time, suggests camping at home instead.
   - C. They hear a typhoon is coming, so they decide to wait until the morning before deciding whether to go camping.

2. **Kenta says: "I think I might be getting a cold. Should I take tomorrow off work?" Tell him to wait and see how he feels for now before deciding.** *(say it)*  
   nanka kaze hiita kamo. ashita, shigoto yasunda hou ga ii kana? (なんか風邪ひいたかも。明日、仕事休んだほうがいいかな？)

3. **What did Sora say about Sunday's football match?**  
   sora-san wa nichiyoubi no sakkaa no shiai ni tsuite, nan to iimashita ka? (ソラさんは日曜日のサッカーの試合について、何と言いましたか？)
   - A. *mou enki ni natta tte.* (Apparently it's already been postponed.)
   - B. *mou kyanseru sareta tte.* (Apparently they've already cancelled it.)
   - C. *mou chuushi ni natta tte.* (Apparently it's already been called off.)

4. **Yuki says: "You're really coming tomorrow at ten, right? Don't be late like last time." Tell her not to worry: this time you'll keep your promise.** *(say it)*  
   ashita no juuji, honto ni kite kureru? mae mitai ni okurenaide ne. (明日の十時、ほんとに来てくれる？前みたいに遅れないでね。)

5. **For the camp at home, what does Sora say he'll do himself?**  
   uchi de kyanpu suru toki, sora-san wa nani o suru to iimashita ka? (うちでキャンプするとき、ソラさんは何をすると言いましたか？)
   - A. Work out the plan for it.
   - B. Put the tent up in the room.
   - C. Do the cooking.

### New words

- **kyanpujou** (キャンプ場): campsite
- **yoyaku** (予約): a booking, a reservation
- **kyanseru suru** (キャンセルする): cancel (a booking, an order, your own plans)
- **tento** (テント): tent

<details>
<summary>Answers and transcript</summary>

1. **B**. Lines 1-2: Kenta reminds Sora to keep his promise this time, and Sora swears he will. Line 4: a typhoon is coming. Line 9: an email from the campsite settles it, kyanpu chuushi ni natta. Line 12: Sora suggests uchi de kyanpu shiyou yo and reminds Kenta he said he'd keep his promise. A: work came up for Sora last time (line 2), not now, and Kenta isn't the one who calls it off. C: Kenta does suggest waiting until the morning (line 5), but the email arrives that same evening, so there's nothing left to wait for (line 10).
2. Tell him to hold off and see how it goes with yousu o miru, not plain matsu: toriaezu konban wa yousu mitara? For example: *toriaezu konban wa yousu mitara?* (Why not just see how you feel tonight for now?); *mada wakannai yo. ashita no asa made yousu mite kara kimetara?* (You can't tell yet. Why not wait and see till tomorrow morning, then decide?). Holding off to see how something develops is yousu o miru, literally 'look at how things are'. Kenta says toriaezu ashita no asa made yousu miyou about the typhoon (line 5), and once the camp is off Sora says ashita made yousu miru hitsuyou mo nakatta ne (line 10). Plain matsu ('wait') says nothing about watching how things go. For a cold, friends and doctors alike say yousu o miru.
3. **C**. Line 6: nichiyoubi no sakkaa no shiai, mou chuushi ni natta tte. A match, a festival or any other event that gets called off is chuushi ni naru, and the camping trip gets the same phrase in line 9. B, kyanseru sareta, is English 'cancelled' word for word. kyanseru is for bookings and your own arrangements, as in Kenta's yoyaku mo kyanseru shinakya (line 9), not for an event that gets called off. A, enki ni natta, is the same frame with a different outcome: put off to a later date, not called off.
4. Promise with yakusoku o mamoru, not motsu: daijoubu, kondo wa chanto yakusoku mamoru yo. For example: *daijoubu, kondo wa chanto yakusoku mamoru yo.* (Don't worry, this time I'll keep my promise properly.); *un, yakusoku wa mamoru kara. juuji ni iku ne.* (Yeah, I'll keep my word. I'll be there at ten.). Keeping a promise is yakusoku o mamoru, literally 'protect the promise'. Kenta reminds Sora with kondo wa chanto yakusoku mamotte yo (line 1), and Sora pledges yakusoku wa mamoru yo (line 2), then holds him to it: yakusoku wa mamoru tte itta desho? (line 12). English 'keep' points to motsu, but Japanese doesn't say that about promises. Since Yuki is worried about lateness, being on time takes the same verb: jikan o mamoru.
5. **A**. Line 14: keikaku wa boku ga tateru. Plans are 'stood up' in Japanese: keikaku o tateru is working out a plan, as Kenta says about his month of planning (sengetsu kara keikaku tateteta, line 3; sekkaku keikaku tateta noni, line 11). B: tateru literally means 'stand something up', and the tent comes up next (tento wa heya no naka), but Sora only says where it goes. The thing he's standing up is the plan. C: the cooking goes to Kenta (gohan wa kenta ga tsukuru), and Kenta notices: sora ga keikaku tateru to, kekkyoku boku ga gohan tsukuru n da (line 15). You tsukuru a meal but tateru a plan.

| # | Speaker | Romaji | English |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Kenta | sora, ashita no kyanpu, honto ni daijoubu? kondo wa chanto yakusoku mamotte yo. | Sora, are you really OK for tomorrow's camping? Keep your promise this time, properly. |
| 2 | Sora | daijoubu da tte. mae wa kyuu ni shigoto haitchatta dake da yo. yakusoku wa mamoru yo. | I told you, it's fine. Last time work just came up out of nowhere. I'll keep my promise. |
| 3 | Kenta | nara ii kedo. sengetsu kara keikaku tateteta n da kara ne. | Good, then. I've been planning this since last month, you know. |
| 4 | Sora | shitteru yo. a, chotto kore mite. ashita, taifuu kuru tte. | I know. Oh, look at this. They say a typhoon's coming tomorrow. |
| 5 | Kenta | e, uso. demo, mada wakannai yo ne. toriaezu ashita no asa made yousu miyou. | What, no way. But we can't tell yet, right? Let's just wait and see till tomorrow morning. |
| 6 | Sora | un. a, mite. nichiyoubi no sakkaa no shiai, mou chuushi ni natta tte. | Yeah. Oh, look. Sunday's football match has already been called off. |
| 7 | Kenta | maji de? a, kyanpujou kara meeru da. | Seriously? Oh, there's an email from the campsite. |
| 8 | Sora | e, nante? | Oh, what's it say? |
| 9 | Kenta | dame da. taifuu de, kyanpu chuushi ni natta. yoyaku mo kyanseru shinakya. | No good. The camping's been called off because of the typhoon. I'll have to cancel the booking too. |
| 10 | Sora | zannen. ashita made yousu miru hitsuyou mo nakatta ne. | Too bad. Guess we didn't even need to wait and see till tomorrow. |
| 11 | Kenta | haa, sekkaku keikaku tateta noni naa. | Sigh. After all the trouble I went to planning it. |
| 12 | Sora | jaa sa, uchi de kyanpu shiyou yo. yakusoku wa mamoru tte itta desho? | Then let's camp at home. I said I'd keep my promise, didn't I? |
| 13 | Kenta | uchi de? ii kedo, keikaku wa? | At home? Sure, but what's the plan? |
| 14 | Sora | keikaku wa boku ga tateru. tento wa heya no naka. de, gohan wa kenta ga tsukuru. | I'll work out the plan. The tent goes indoors. And you do the cooking. |
| 15 | Kenta | sora ga keikaku tateru to, kekkyoku boku ga gohan tsukuru n da. | So when Sora makes the plans, I end up doing the cooking anyway. |
| 16 | Sora | ii jan. kenta no gohan, oishii shi. | So what? Your cooking's good. |
| 17 | Kenta | wakatta, wakatta. jaa, taifuu ga kite mo, uchi no kyanpu wa chuushi ni naranai ne. | Okay, okay. Well, at least our home camp won't get called off, even if the typhoon comes. |

</details>

## 83. Tanaka-san dresses up

*Hats and accessories · Mixed dialogue · 18 lines · Yuki, Mai, Tanaka-san*

A hot weekday morning at the office. Mai tells Yuki that their manager, Tanaka-san, has come in unusually dressed up, and the two of them try to work out why, until he walks over.

**Collocations:** *boushi o kaburu* (wear / put on a hat); *yubiwa o suru* (wear a ring); *nekutai o suru* (wear a tie); *kousui o tsukeru* (wear / put on perfume).

**Review:** *kakko tsukeru* (show off, act cool); *tenshon ga agaru* (get excited, get hyped).

### Audio script

| Speaker | Voice |
|---|---|
| Yuki | female, late 20s, bright and friendly |
| Mai | female, late 20s, warm, a little shy |
| Tanaka-san | male, 40s, calm and kind, polite office speech |

| # | Speaker | Japanese | Kana (if the voice misreads a kanji) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Mai | ねえ、ユキ。今朝の田中さん、見た？ | ねえ、ゆき。けさのたなかさん、みた？ |
| 2 | Yuki | ううん、まだ。どうしたの？ | ううん、まだ。どうしたの？ |
| 3 | Mai | 帽子かぶってきたの。初めて見たよ。 | ぼうしかぶってきたの。はじめてみたよ。 |
| 4 | Yuki | え、田中さんって帽子かぶるんだ。 | え、たなかさんってぼうしかぶるんだ。 |
| 5 | Mai | それに、ネクタイもしてるの。いつもはしてないのに。 | それに、ねくたいもしてるの。いつもはしてないのに。 |
| 6 | Yuki | え、こんなに暑いのに？何かあるのかな。 | え、こんなにあついのに？なにかあるのかな。 |
| 7 | Mai | あとね、香水もつけてた。すごくいい匂いしたよ。 | あとね、こうすいもつけてた。すごくいいにおいしたよ。 |
| 8 | Yuki | 田中さんが香水つけるなんて！もしかして、デート？ | たなかさんがこうすいつけるなんて！もしかして、でーと？ |
| 9 | Mai | でも田中さん、結婚してるよ。 | でもたなかさん、けっこんしてるよ。 |
| 10 | Yuki | あ、そうだった。でも指輪してるの、見たことないかも。 | あ、そうだった。でもゆびわしてるの、みたことないかも。 |
| 11 | Mai | それがね、今日は指輪もしてるの。 | それがね、きょうはゆびわもしてるの。 |
| 12 | Yuki | 帽子かぶって、ネクタイして、香水つけて、指輪まで？絶対何かあるよ！ | ぼうしかぶって、ねくたいして、こうすいつけて、ゆびわまで？ぜったいなにかあるよ！ |
| 13 | Tanaka-san | おはようございます。何かあったんですか？ | おはようございます。なにかあったんですか？ |
| 14 | Yuki | あ、おはようございます！あの、今日はネクタイしてるんですね。 | あ、おはようございます！あの、きょうはねくたいしてるんですね。 |
| 15 | Tanaka-san | ああ、これですか。実は今日で、結婚して十年なんです。 | ああ、これですか。じつはきょうで、けっこんしてじゅうねんなんです。 |
| 16 | Mai | おめでとうございます！だから指輪もしてきたんですね。 | おめでとうございます！だからゆびわもしてきたんですね。 |
| 17 | Tanaka-san | ええ。今晩は妻とデートなので、ちょっとかっこつけてみました。 | ええ。こんばんはつまとでーとなので、ちょっとかっこつけてみました。 |
| 18 | Yuki | やっぱりデートだったんだ！なんか私までテンション上がってきた。 | やっぱりでーとだったんだ！なんかわたしまでてんしょんあがってきた。 |

### Questions

1. **Why has Tanaka-san come in dressed up today?**  
   tanaka-san wa kyou, nani ga aru n desu ka? (田中さんは今日、何があるんですか？)
   - A. He's going to a friend's wedding after work.
   - B. He's meeting an important client at the office today.
   - C. It's ten years since he got married, and he has a date with his wife tonight.

2. **Mai tells Yuki what Tanaka-san came in wearing this morning. What did she say?**  
   mai-san wa, kesa no tanaka-san ni tsuite nan to iimashita ka? (マイさんは、今朝の田中さんについて何と言いましたか？)
   - A. *boushi kite kita no* (He came in wearing a hat (kiru, the clothes verb).)
   - B. *boushi kabutte kita no* (He came in with a hat on (kaburu).)
   - C. *boushi tsukete kita no* (He came in with a hat put on (tsukeru).)

3. **Yuki says she's never seen Tanaka-san wear a ring. What does Mai tell her?**  
   mai-san wa, tanaka-san no yubiwa ni tsuite nan to iimashita ka? (マイさんは、田中さんの指輪について何と言いましたか？)
   - A. Today he's wearing his ring, too.
   - B. He's bought a ring to give his wife tonight.
   - C. He's left his ring at home again today.

4. **A few days later Tanaka-san is back in his usual open-necked shirt. He has to meet someone important tomorrow, and he asks you, "ashita, daiji na hito ni au n desu kedo, kore de daijoubu deshou ka?" Suggest, politely, that he wear a tie.** *(say it)*  
   ashita, daiji na hito ni au n desu kedo, kore de daijoubu deshou ka? (明日、大事な人に会うんですけど、これで大丈夫でしょうか？)

5. **You've just got to the office. Mai leans over and says, "are, nanka ii nioi suru ne." Tell her you've got perfume on.** *(say it)*  
   are, nanka ii nioi suru ne. (あれ、なんかいい匂いするね。)

### New words

- **kekkon** (結婚): marriage (kekkon shiteru: is married)
- **deeto** (デート): a date
- **tsuma** (妻): my wife
- **nioi** (匂い): smell (ii nioi: a nice smell)

<details>
<summary>Answers and transcript</summary>

1. **C**. Line 15: kyou de kekkon shite juu nen (today makes ten years since he married), and line 17: konban wa tsuma to deeto (a date with his wife tonight), so he dressed up a little (chotto kakko tsukete mimashita). Yuki's guess of a date was right, just not the way she meant it. A: kekkon is his own marriage, not a friend's wedding. B: nobody mentions a client or a meeting.
2. **B**. Line 3: 'boushi kabutte kita no', and Yuki echoes it in line 4: boushi kaburu n da. Anything pulled over the head (a hat, a cap, a helmet) takes kaburu. A: kiru is for clothes on the body, like a shirt or a coat, so boushi o kiru is the English 'wear' carried over. C: tsukeru is for things put onto the body, like the perfume he also had on (kousui tsuketeta), not for a hat.
3. **A**. Line 11: 'sore ga ne, kyou wa yubiwa mo shiteru no': today he has his ring on as well as the hat, tie and perfume. yubiwa shiteru means the ring is on his finger; nothing is said about buying one (B) or leaving it at home (C). Mai says it to him in line 16 too: dakara yubiwa mo shite kita n desu ne (so that's why you came in wearing your ring).
4. Suggest a tie with nekutai o suru, politely since he's your manager: nekutai o shite itta hou ga ii to omoimasu, or more softly, nekutai shite itta hou ga ii kamo shiremasen ne. Skip -tara dou desu ka with a manager, as it can sound pushy. A tie takes suru, never kiru. For example: *ichiou, nekutai o shite itta hou ga ii to omoimasu.* (I think you'd better wear a tie, just in case.); *kono aida mitai ni, nekutai shite itta hou ga ii kamo shiremasen ne.* (Maybe wear a tie, like you did the other day.). A tie, like a ring or a watch, takes suru: nekutai shite iku is 'go wearing a tie', nekutai shite kuru 'come in wearing one'. In the scene: nekutai mo shiteru no (line 5) and kyou wa nekutai shiteru n desu ne (line 14). nekutai o kiru is the English 'wear' carried over; kiru is only for clothes on the body.
5. Say you're wearing perfume with kousui o tsukeru: kousui tsuketeru (it's on) or kousui tsukete kita (I put some on before I came). Not kousui o kiru. For example: *a, kousui tsuketeru kara kamo.* (Oh, maybe it's my perfume.); *kyou wa chotto kousui tsukete kita n da.* (I put on a bit of perfume today.). Perfume takes tsukeru, the verb for anything put onto the body, and tsuketeru means it's on, not that you're putting it on now. In the scene: kousui mo tsuketeta (line 7) and kousui tsukeru nante (line 8). kiru is only for clothes.

| # | Speaker | Romaji | English |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Mai | nee, yuki. kesa no tanaka-san, mita? | Hey, Yuki. Did you see Tanaka-san this morning? |
| 2 | Yuki | uun, mada. dou shita no? | No, not yet. Why, what's up with him? |
| 3 | Mai | boushi kabutte kita no. hajimete mita yo. | He came in wearing a hat. I've never seen that before. |
| 4 | Yuki | e, tanaka-san tte boushi kaburu n da. | Huh, I didn't know Tanaka-san wore hats. |
| 5 | Mai | sore ni, nekutai mo shiteru no. itsumo wa shitenai noni. | And he's got a tie on, too. He never wears one usually. |
| 6 | Yuki | e, konna ni atsui noni? nanika aru no kana. | What, in this heat? I wonder if something's going on. |
| 7 | Mai | ato ne, kousui mo tsuketeta. sugoku ii nioi shita yo. | Oh, and he had perfume on, too. He smelled really nice. |
| 8 | Yuki | tanaka-san ga kousui tsukeru nante! moshikashite, deeto? | Tanaka-san wearing perfume! Don't tell me he's got a date? |
| 9 | Mai | demo tanaka-san, kekkon shiteru yo. | But Tanaka-san's married. |
| 10 | Yuki | a, sou datta. demo yubiwa shiteru no, mita koto nai kamo. | Oh, right. But I don't think I've ever seen him wear a ring. |
| 11 | Mai | sore ga ne, kyou wa yubiwa mo shiteru no. | Well, that's the thing. Today he's wearing his ring, too. |
| 12 | Yuki | boushi kabutte, nekutai shite, kousui tsukete, yubiwa made? zettai nanika aru yo! | A hat, a tie, perfume, and his ring as well? Something's definitely going on! |
| 13 | Tanaka-san | ohayou gozaimasu. nanika atta n desu ka? | Good morning. Has something happened? |
| 14 | Yuki | a, ohayou gozaimasu! ano, kyou wa nekutai shiteru n desu ne. | Oh, good morning! Um, I see you're wearing a tie today. |
| 15 | Tanaka-san | aa, kore desu ka. jitsu wa kyou de, kekkon shite juu nen na n desu. | Ah, this? Actually, today it's ten years since I got married. |
| 16 | Mai | omedetou gozaimasu! dakara yubiwa mo shite kita n desu ne. | Congratulations! So that's why you've got your ring on, too. |
| 17 | Tanaka-san | ee. konban wa tsuma to deeto na node, chotto kakko tsukete mimashita. | Yes. I've got a date with my wife tonight, so I thought I'd try to look sharp. |
| 18 | Yuki | yappari deeto datta n da! nanka watashi made tenshon agatte kita. | I knew it was a date! Now I'm all excited too. |

</details>

## 84. A job close to home

*University and jobs · Polite dialogue · 18 lines · Mai, Tanaka-san*

Lunchtime at the office in late March. Mai (Kimura-san at work) is about to heat up her packed lunch in the kitchenette microwave when her manager, Tanaka-san, comes in with news about his son, who has spent the past year job hunting.

**Collocations:** *shigoto o sagasu* (look for a job); *shigoto ga mitsukaru* (find a job); *daigaku ni hairu* (get into university, start university); *kaisha ni hairu* (join a company); *keiken ga aru* (have experience).

**Review:** *obentou o atatameru* (heat up a boxed lunch); *haji o kaku* (embarrass yourself, make a fool of yourself).

### Audio script

| Speaker | Voice |
|---|---|
| Mai | female, late 20s, warm, a little shy |
| Tanaka-san | male, 40s, calm and kind, polite office speech |

| # | Speaker | Japanese | Kana (if the voice misreads a kanji) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Mai | あ、田中さん、お疲れさまです。先にお弁当を温めてもいいですか？ | あ、たなかさん、おつかれさまです。さきにおべんとうをあたためてもいいですか？ |
| 2 | Tanaka-san | どうぞ。あ、木村さん、うちの息子、やっと仕事が見つかったんですよ。 | どうぞ。あ、きむらさん、うちのむすこ、やっとしごとがみつかったんですよ。 |
| 3 | Mai | わあ、おめでとうございます！去年からずっと仕事を探してたんですよね。 | わあ、おめでとうございます！きょねんからずっとしごとをさがしてたんですよね。 |
| 4 | Tanaka-san | ええ。一年も仕事探して、なかなか見つからなかったんです。 | ええ。いちねんもしごとさがして、なかなかみつからなかったんです。 |
| 5 | Mai | 仕事が見つかって、ほんとによかったですね。どこの会社に入るんですか？ | しごとがみつかって、ほんとによかったですね。どこのかいしゃにはいるんですか？ |
| 6 | Tanaka-san | それが、来月から、うちの会社に入るんです。 | それが、らいげつから、うちのかいしゃにはいるんです。 |
| 7 | Mai | え、この会社にですか？全然知りませんでした。 | え、このかいしゃにですか？ぜんぜんしりませんでした。 |
| 8 | Tanaka-san | 私も昨日まで知らなかったんですよ。息子が自分で仕事を探して、私には何も言わなかったんです。 | わたしもきのうまでしらなかったんですよ。むすこがじぶんでしごとをさがして、わたしにはなにもいわなかったんです。 |
| 9 | Mai | すごいですね。息子さんが大学に入ったの、この間じゃなかったですか？ | すごいですね。むすこさんがだいがくにはいったの、このあいだじゃなかったですか？ |
| 10 | Tanaka-san | ええ、早いですね。でも、大学に入ってから勉強ばっかりで、仕事の経験が全然ないんです。 | ええ、はやいですね。でも、だいがくにはいってからべんきょうばっかりで、しごとのけいけんがぜんぜんないんです。 |
| 11 | Mai | 大丈夫ですよ。最初から経験がある人は、ほとんどいませんから。 | だいじょうぶですよ。さいしょからけいけんがあるひとは、ほとんどいませんから。 |
| 12 | Tanaka-san | そうでしょうか。木村さんも、最初は経験がなかったんですか？ | そうでしょうか。きむらさんも、さいしょはけいけんがなかったんですか？ |
| 13 | Mai | はい。この会社に入ったときは、何もできなくて、毎日恥をかいていました。 | はい。このかいしゃにはいったときは、なにもできなくて、まいにちはじをかいていました。 |
| 14 | Tanaka-san | そうでしたか？でも今は、みんな、わからないことは木村さんに聞いていますよ。 | そうでしたか？でもいまは、みんな、わからないことはきむらさんにきいていますよ。 |
| 15 | Mai | そんなことないですよ。息子さんが困っていたら、私もいろいろ教えますね。 | そんなことないですよ。むすこさんがこまっていたら、わたしもいろいろおしえますね。 |
| 16 | Tanaka-san | ありがとうございます。あ、でも、私の息子だってことは、まだみんなに言わないでくださいね。 | ありがとうございます。あ、でも、わたしのむすこだってことは、まだみんなにいわないでくださいね。 |
| 17 | Mai | え、なんでですか？ | え、なんでですか？ |
| 18 | Tanaka-san | 自分で入った会社だから言わないでって、息子に言われたんです。 | じぶんではいったかいしゃだからいわないでって、むすこにいわれたんです。 |

### Questions

1. **What happens in this conversation?**  
   nani ga arimashita ka? (何がありましたか？)
   - A. Tanaka-san's son is still looking for work after a year, so Tanaka-san asks Mai whether their company might take him on.
   - B. Tanaka-san's son has just got into university, and Mai offers to help him get some work experience before he starts.
   - C. Tanaka-san's son has finally found a job, at their own company, and Tanaka-san asks Mai to keep quiet for now that he's his son.

2. **At Yuki's birthday party, her manager Tanaka-san makes small talk: "What kind of work are you doing at the moment?" Tell him that, actually, you're looking for a job right now.** *(say it)*  
   ima, donna oshigoto o shite iru n desu ka? (今、どんなお仕事をしているんですか？)

3. **Mai asks you: "Oh yeah, how's the job thing going? Are you still looking?" Tell her no, you've finally found a job.** *(say it)*  
   sou ieba, shigoto dou? mada sagashiteru no? (そういえば、仕事どう？まだ探してるの？)

4. **Mai remembers when Tanaka-san's son started university. What did she say?**  
   mai-san wa, musuko-san ni tsuite nan to iimashita ka? (マイさんは、息子さんについて何と言いましたか？)
   - A. *daigaku ni haitta* (that he started university (got in))
   - B. *daigaku o hajimeta* (that he began university (English 'start' word for word))
   - C. *daigaku o haitta* (that he entered university (with o, like English 'enter'))

5. **Tanaka-san's news is that his son will uchi no kaisha ni hairu from next month. What does that mean?**  
   tanaka-san no musuko-san wa, raigetsu kara nani o shimasu ka? (田中さんの息子さんは、来月から何をしますか？)
   - A. He'll come into the office with his dad now and then to see what the work is like.
   - B. He'll join their company and start working there as a new employee.
   - C. He'll take part in a short work-experience event that their company runs.

6. **Tanaka-san, your manager, asks: "Could I ask you to take on this job? It's a little tricky." Tell him it's fine, because you have experience with this kind of work.** *(say it)*  
   kono shigoto, onegai shite mo ii desu ka? chotto muzukashii n desu kedo. (この仕事、お願いしてもいいですか？ちょっと難しいんですけど。)

### New words

- **musuko** (息子): son (musuko-san: someone else's son)

<details>
<summary>Answers and transcript</summary>

1. **C**. Line 2: uchi no musuko, yatto shigoto ga mitsukatta n desu yo. Line 6: the job is at their own company, uchi no kaisha ni hairu n desu. Lines 16-18: Tanaka-san asks Mai not to tell anyone yet that he's his son, because his son wants it known he got in on his own. A: the son did spend a year looking (line 4), but the search is over. B: university only comes up as Mai remembering when he started (line 9); he's finishing now, and the experience talk is Tanaka-san worrying that he has none (line 10).
2. Say you're job hunting with shigoto o sagasu in the -te iru form, politely: jitsu wa, ima shigoto o sagashite iru n desu. For example: *jitsu wa, ima shigoto o sagashite iru n desu.* (Actually, I'm looking for a job right now.); *ima wa, atarashii shigoto o sagashite imasu.* (At the moment I'm looking for a new job.). Looking for work is shigoto o sagasu, and an ongoing search takes -te iru: sagashite iru, or sagashiteru in casual speech. Mai uses it about Tanaka-san's son, kyonen kara zutto shigoto o sagashiteta n desu yo ne (line 3), Tanaka-san adds ichinen mo shigoto sagashite (line 4), and his son found the job jibun de shigoto o sagashite, looking on his own (line 8). Sagasu is only the looking: once the search is over, the job takes ga, shigoto ga mitsukaru.
3. Say the search is over with shigoto ga mitsukaru in the past: uun, yatto shigoto mitsukatta n da! For example: *uun, yatto shigoto mitsukatta n da!* (No, I finally found a job!); *uun, sengetsu atarashii shigoto ga mitsukatta n da yo.* (No, I found a new job last month.). Finding work is shigoto ga mitsukaru: the job is what turns up, so it takes ga, not the o of sagasu. Tanaka-san's news is uchi no musuko, yatto shigoto ga mitsukatta n desu yo (line 2), and Mai answers shigoto ga mitsukatte, honto ni yokatta desu ne (line 5). Mai's question uses sagasu for the looking; your answer switches both the verb and the particle. Casual speech can drop ga, as in the first model answer.
4. **A**. Mai says musuko-san ga daigaku ni haitta no, kono aida ja nakatta desu ka? (line 9), and Tanaka-san picks it up: daigaku ni haitte kara benkyou bakkari (line 10). You go into a school, so starting university is daigaku ni hairu, with ni for the place you go into, the same ni hairu as kaisha ni hairu. B puts English 'start university' into Japanese word for word, and nobody says it. C copies English 'enter university' with o, but hairu takes ni for what you join: daigaku o haitta is a common learner slip.
5. **B**. Line 6: sore ga, raigetsu kara, uchi no kaisha ni hairu n desu. Kaisha ni hairu is joining a company as a member of staff: Mai uses it for her own start, kono kaisha ni haitta toki (line 13), and the son wants it known he got in on his own, jibun de haitta kaisha (line 18). Going in to the office is kaisha ni iku or kuru (A), and taking part in an event is sanka suru (C), the word English 'join' tempts you into.
6. Say you have the background with keiken ga aru, politely keiken ga arimasu, not keiken o motte imasu: kou iu shigoto no keiken wa arimasu kara, daijoubu desu. For example: *hai, kou iu shigoto no keiken wa arimasu kara, daijoubu desu.* (Sure. I've done this kind of work before, so it's fine.); *daijoubu desu. mae ni mo onaji shigoto o shita keiken ga arimasu.* (No problem. I've done the same work before.). Experience is something that exists, so it takes ga aru: keiken ga aru, or keiken ga arimasu to your manager. Tanaka-san worries his son has none, shigoto no keiken ga zenzen nai n desu (line 10); Mai answers saisho kara keiken ga aru hito wa, hotondo imasen (line 11); and Tanaka-san asks her, saisho wa keiken ga nakatta n desu ka? (line 12). Keiken o motte imasu is English 'have experience' word for word and sounds like a line read off a CV. Wa in place of ga (keiken wa arimasu) is fine when you mean this kind of work, at least.

| # | Speaker | Romaji | English |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Mai | a, tanaka-san, otsukaresama desu. saki ni obentou o atatamete mo ii desu ka? | Oh, hi, Tanaka-san. Mind if I heat up my lunch first? |
| 2 | Tanaka-san | douzo. a, kimura-san, uchi no musuko, yatto shigoto ga mitsukatta n desu yo. | Go ahead. Oh, Kimura-san, guess what: my son's finally found a job! |
| 3 | Mai | waa, omedetou gozaimasu! kyonen kara zutto shigoto o sagashiteta n desu yo ne. | Oh, congratulations! He'd been looking for a job since last year, hadn't he? |
| 4 | Tanaka-san | ee. ichinen mo shigoto sagashite, nakanaka mitsukaranakatta n desu. | Yes. A whole year of job hunting, and nothing would turn up. |
| 5 | Mai | shigoto ga mitsukatte, honto ni yokatta desu ne. doko no kaisha ni hairu n desu ka? | I'm so glad he's found one. Which company is he joining? |
| 6 | Tanaka-san | sore ga, raigetsu kara, uchi no kaisha ni hairu n desu. | Well, that's the thing. Next month he's joining our company. |
| 7 | Mai | e, kono kaisha ni desu ka? zenzen shirimasen deshita. | What, this company? I had no idea. |
| 8 | Tanaka-san | watashi mo kinou made shiranakatta n desu yo. musuko ga jibun de shigoto o sagashite, watashi ni wa nanimo iwanakatta n desu. | Neither did I, until yesterday. He looked for work all on his own and never said a word to me. |
| 9 | Mai | sugoi desu ne. musuko-san ga daigaku ni haitta no, kono aida ja nakatta desu ka? | Good for him. Didn't he only just start university the other day? |
| 10 | Tanaka-san | ee, hayai desu ne. demo, daigaku ni haitte kara benkyou bakkari de, shigoto no keiken ga zenzen nai n desu. | Yes, time flies. But since he started university it's been nothing but studying, so he's got no work experience at all. |
| 11 | Mai | daijoubu desu yo. saisho kara keiken ga aru hito wa, hotondo imasen kara. | He'll be fine. Hardly anyone has experience at the start. |
| 12 | Tanaka-san | sou deshou ka. kimura-san mo, saisho wa keiken ga nakatta n desu ka? | You think so? So you had no experience at first either, Kimura-san? |
| 13 | Mai | hai. kono kaisha ni haitta toki wa, nanimo dekinakute, mainichi haji o kaite imashita. | That's right. When I joined this company I couldn't do a thing, and I was making a fool of myself every day. |
| 14 | Tanaka-san | sou deshita ka? demo ima wa, minna, wakaranai koto wa kimura-san ni kiite imasu yo. | Were you? Well, these days everyone comes to you when they don't know something. |
| 15 | Mai | sonna koto nai desu yo. musuko-san ga komatte itara, watashi mo iroiro oshiemasu ne. | Oh, not really. If your son ever gets stuck, I'll help him out too. |
| 16 | Tanaka-san | arigatou gozaimasu. a, demo, watashi no musuko da tte koto wa, mada minna ni iwanaide kudasai ne. | Thank you. Oh, but please don't tell anyone yet that he's my son. |
| 17 | Mai | e, nande desu ka? | Oh? Why not? |
| 18 | Tanaka-san | jibun de haitta kaisha da kara iwanaide tte, musuko ni iwareta n desu. | My son told me not to. He got into this company on his own, he says. |

## 85. Pale, then red

*Visible signs · Casual monologue · 12 lines · Okaasan*

A Sunday evening. Okaasan leaves a voicemail for Yuki, who lives on her own, about Yuki's little brother Kenji and his football match that day.

**Collocations:** *kega o suru* (get hurt, get injured); *chi ga deru* (bleed, be bleeding); *ase o kaku* (sweat, get sweaty); *kaoiro ga warui* (look pale, look unwell); *kao ga akai* (be red in the face).

**Review:** *netsu ga aru* (have a fever); *kakko tsukeru* (show off, act cool).

### Audio script

| Speaker | Voice |
|---|---|
| Okaasan | female, 50s, warm, motherly |

| # | Speaker | Japanese | Kana (if the voice misreads a kanji) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Okaasan | もしもし、ユキ？お母さんだけど。 | もしもし、ゆき？おかあさんだけど。 |
| 2 | Okaasan | ケンジがね、今日サッカーの試合で怪我したの。あ、大丈夫だから、心配しないでね。 | けんじがね、きょうさっかーのしあいでけがしたの。あ、だいじょうぶだから、しんぱいしないでね。 |
| 3 | Okaasan | 帰ってきたとき、膝から血が出てて、汗もすごくかいててね。 | かえってきたとき、ひざからちがでてて、あせもすごくかいててね。 |
| 4 | Okaasan | 顔色も悪かったし、お母さん、ほんとにびっくりしたわ。 | かおいろもわるかったし、おかあさん、ほんとにびっくりしたわ。 |
| 5 | Okaasan | 「ひどい怪我したんじゃないの？」って聞いたら、「ちょっと血が出ただけだよ」って。 | 「ひどいけがしたんじゃないの？」ってきいたら、「ちょっとちがでただけだよ」って。 |
| 6 | Okaasan | そういえば、ケンジって昔から、血が出るとすぐ顔色が悪くなるのよね。 | そういえば、けんじってむかしから、ちがでるとすぐかおいろがわるくなるのよね。 |
| 7 | Okaasan | で、夜になって、試合を見に来てた女の子が、ケンジの忘れ物を持ってきてくれたの。 | で、よるになって、しあいをみにきてたおんなのこが、けんじのわすれものをもってきてくれたの。 |
| 8 | Okaasan | そしたらケンジ、急に顔が赤くなっちゃって。 | そしたらけんじ、きゅうにかおがあかくなっちゃって。 |
| 9 | Okaasan | 昼は顔色が悪かったのに、今度は顔が赤いのよ。 | ひるはかおいろがわるかったのに、こんどはかおがあかいのよ。 |
| 10 | Okaasan | あとで、「顔赤いよ、熱あるの？」って聞いたら、「暑いだけだよ」って。また汗かいてたわ。 | あとで、「かおあかいよ、ねつあるの？」ってきいたら、「あついだけだよ」って。またあせかいてたわ。 |
| 11 | Okaasan | 怪我したのも、あの子の前でかっこつけたからかもね。 | けがしたのも、あのこのまえでかっこつけたからかもね。 |
| 12 | Okaasan | ケンジには、お母さんから聞いたって言わないでね。じゃあね。 | けんじには、おかあさんからきいたっていわないでね。じゃあね。 |

### Questions

1. **What's Okaasan's message about?**  
   nani ga arimashita ka? (何がありましたか？)
   - A. Kenji hurt his knee at football and came down with a fever that evening, so he's resting.
   - B. Kenji fainted at the sight of blood during his match, and a girl had to bring him home.
   - C. Kenji got a small cut at football, and Okaasan thinks his red face that evening was about a girl, not a fever.

2. **You're home for the weekend with a plaster on your knee. Okaasan spots it and asks, "are, sono hiza dou shita no?" Tell her you hurt it, using kega.** *(say it)*  
   are, sono hiza dou shita no? (あれ、その膝どうしたの？)

3. **Okaasan asked Kenji if he'd hurt himself badly. What did he say?**  
   kenji wa nan to iimashita ka? (ケンジは何と言いましたか？)
   - A. *chotto chi ga deta dake da yo* (It just bled a bit, that's all.)
   - B. *chotto shukketsu shita dake da yo* (There was just a bit of haemorrhaging, that's all.)
   - C. *chotto chi o dashita dake da yo* (I just let a bit of blood out, that's all.)

4. **You've just walked in after an errand on a hot afternoon. Okaasan asks, "soto, atsukatta?" Answer using ase o kaku, and say you want a shower.** *(say it)*  
   soto, atsukatta? (外、暑かった？)

5. **Okaasan sits down at the kitchen table and says, "nanka kesa kara, chotto kibun ga warui no yo ne." She has gone pale. Tell her she looks pale, and ask if she's OK.** *(say it)*  
   nanka kesa kara, chotto kibun ga warui no yo ne. (なんか今朝から、ちょっと気分が悪いのよね。)

6. **Okaasan compares how Kenji looked at midday with how he looked in the evening, when the girl came round. What changed?**  
   kenji no kao wa, dou kawarimashita ka? (ケンジの顔は、どう変わりましたか？)
   - A. At midday he was red from the heat, and in the evening he went pale.
   - B. At midday he looked pale, and in the evening his face went red.
   - C. He looked pale all day, and in the evening he came down with a fever.

### New words

- **hiza** (膝): knee
- **onna no ko** (女の子): girl (ano ko = that girl)
- **wasuremono** (忘れ物): something left behind

<details>
<summary>Answers and transcript</summary>

1. **C**. Line 2: Kenji got hurt at his football match (kega shita), but Okaasan says he's fine. Line 5: he says it only bled a little. Lines 7-9: when a girl who'd watched the match brought round something he'd left behind, his face suddenly went red. Line 10: he says he's just hot, and in line 11 Okaasan guesses he got hurt showing off in front of her. A is wrong: Okaasan asks about a fever (line 10, netsu aru no?), but Kenji says he's just hot, and nobody says he's resting. B is wrong: Kenji went pale at the blood (lines 4 and 6), but he came home himself (line 3, kaette kita), and the girl only brought round something he'd left behind (line 7).
2. Say you hurt it with kega o suru, usually as kega shita, or kega shichatta (I went and got hurt). It's natural to say how it happened: jitensha de kega shita. For example: *a, kore? kinou jitensha de kega shichatta.* (Oh, this? I hurt it on my bike yesterday.); *chotto kega shita dake. mou daijoubu da yo.* (I just hurt it a bit. It's fine now.). Okaasan says Kenji kega shita at his match (line 2), asks him hidoi kega shita n ja nai no? (line 5), and guesses why he got hurt: kega shita no mo... (line 11). In Japanese an injury is something you 'do': kega o suru, even by accident, mostly heard as kega shita or kega shichatta. Itaku natta only says it started to hurt, and kizutsuita is mostly for hurt feelings. To name the place, say hiza o kega shita or hiza ni kega o shita, never two o's (not hiza o kega o shita). With your mum, casual is right.
3. **A**. Line 5: 'chotto chi ga deta dake da yo'. Japanese has no single verb for 'bleed': blood comes out, chi ga deru, the way a fever or a cough does (netsu ga deru, seki ga deru). Okaasan uses the same pair in line 3 (hiza kara chi ga detete) and line 6 (chi ga deru to). B is wrong: shukketsu suru is the medical word for bleeding. A doctor or the news would say it, but not a teenager telling his mum it's nothing. C is wrong: dasu means putting something out yourself, so chi o dashita sounds as if he made it bleed on purpose. Blood from a cut just comes out (deru).
4. Say you got all sweaty with ase o kaku, usually in the past: ase kaita or ase kaichatta. Then add that you want a shower (shawaa abitai). For example: *un, metcha ase kaichatta. shawaa abitai.* (Yeah, I got so sweaty. I want a shower.); *atsukatta! ase kaita kara, saki ni shawaa abiru ne.* (It was hot! I'm all sweaty, so I'll have a shower first.). Okaasan uses it twice. Kenji came home from the match all sweaty (line 3, ase mo sugoku kaitete), and he was sweating again when she asked about his red face (line 10, mata ase kaiteta). 'Sweat' is a verb in English, but Japanese uses the noun ase with kaku, so don't say ase suru. This kaku isn't kaku 'write'. It's the same kaku as in haji o kaku (embarrass yourself). Ase ga deru (sweat comes out) also works, but ase kaita is what people usually say. With your mum, casual is right.
5. Tell her she looks pale with kaoiro ga warui (casually kaoiro warui yo), then ask daijoubu? For example: *honto da, kaoiro warui yo. daijoubu?* (You're right, you look pale. Are you OK?); *kaoiro ga warui ne. chotto yoko ni nattara?* (You do look pale. Why don't you lie down for a bit?). Okaasan says it about Kenji three times: kaoiro mo warukatta (line 4), sugu kaoiro ga waruku naru (line 6) and hiru wa kaoiro ga warukatta (line 9). Kaoiro is the colour of your face, and warui says it's a bad colour: pale, unwell. Kao ga shiroi would sound as if she has fair skin, and kao ga aoi is stronger (looking really ill or scared). Her kibun ga warui is how she feels inside. Kaoiro ga warui is what you can see. In casual talk ga often drops (kaoiro warui yo), and daijoubu? usually follows.
6. **B**. Line 9: 'hiru wa kaoiro ga warukatta noni, kondo wa kao ga akai no yo'. Kaoiro ga warui means pale (from the blood, lines 4 and 6). Kao ga akai is a red face, which came on suddenly when the girl turned up (line 8, kyuu ni kao ga akaku natchatte). A swaps the two. C is wrong: Okaasan does ask kao akai yo, netsu aru no? (line 10), but Kenji says he's just hot, and she thinks it was about the girl (line 11).

| # | Speaker | Romaji | English |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Okaasan | moshimoshi, yuki? okaasan da kedo. | Hello, Yuki? It's Mum. |
| 2 | Okaasan | kenji ga ne, kyou sakkaa no shiai de kega shita no. a, daijoubu da kara, shinpai shinaide ne. | Kenji got hurt in his football match today. Oh, he's fine, so don't worry. |
| 3 | Okaasan | kaette kita toki, hiza kara chi ga detete, ase mo sugoku kaitete ne. | When he got home, his knee was bleeding, and he was really sweaty too. |
| 4 | Okaasan | kaoiro mo warukatta shi, okaasan, honto ni bikkuri shita wa. | And he looked pale, so I got quite a fright. |
| 5 | Okaasan | hidoi kega shita n ja nai no? tte kiitara, chotto chi ga deta dake da yo tte. | I asked him, "You haven't hurt yourself badly, have you?" and he said, "It just bled a bit, that's all." |
| 6 | Okaasan | sou ieba, kenji tte mukashi kara, chi ga deru to sugu kaoiro ga waruku naru no yo ne. | Come to think of it, Kenji's always been like that. The moment he bleeds, he goes pale. |
| 7 | Okaasan | de, yoru ni natte, shiai o mi ni kiteta onna no ko ga, kenji no wasuremono o motte kite kureta no. | Then in the evening, a girl who'd come to watch the match brought round something Kenji had left behind. |
| 8 | Okaasan | soshitara kenji, kyuu ni kao ga akaku natchatte. | And Kenji's face suddenly went red. |
| 9 | Okaasan | hiru wa kaoiro ga warukatta noni, kondo wa kao ga akai no yo. | At midday he'd been pale, and now his face was red! |
| 10 | Okaasan | ato de, kao akai yo, netsu aru no? tte kiitara, atsui dake da yo tte. mata ase kaiteta wa. | Later I said, "Your face is red. Have you got a fever?" and he said, "I'm just hot." He was all sweaty again. |
| 11 | Okaasan | kega shita no mo, ano ko no mae de kakko tsuketa kara kamo ne. | Maybe he even got hurt showing off in front of her. |
| 12 | Okaasan | kenji ni wa, okaasan kara kiita tte iwanaide ne. jaa ne. | Don't tell Kenji you heard it from me, OK? Bye. |

</details>

## 86. Blossoms Twice

*Seasons · Casual dialogue · 17 lines · Yuki, Kenji*

A Sunday in early March at the family home. Yuki, who lives on her own, has dropped by and asks her little brother Kenji, who finishes high school this month, about the university in Hokkaido he starts at in April.

**Collocations:** *hana ga saku* (flowers bloom, come out); *atsusa ni yowai* (can't take the heat); *semi ga naku* (cicadas sing, buzz); *yuki ga tsumoru* (snow settles, lies on the ground).

**Review:** *daigaku ni hairu* (get into university, start university); *ase o kaku* (sweat, get sweaty); *netto de shiraberu* (look something up online).

### Audio script

| Speaker | Voice |
|---|---|
